c_{i,n}x^{m(n-1-i)-(n-1)}+T_{i}^{n}(x)+R_{i}^{n}\left(x,\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}],\dots,\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}^{(i)}\right]\right)(x)
where
c_{i,n}
is a real number,
T_{i}^{n}
is polynomial of degree strictly lower than
m(n-1-i)-(n-1)
and
R_{i}^{n}
is also a polynomial of degree greater than
1
. Since all these Hilbert transform vanish at infinity, such a polynomial expression goes to zero at infinity. This decomposition holds as long as the degree of the monomial is non-negative, otherwise it is zero. We give the first decompositions for
\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]\right)^{(k)}
for
k\in\llbracket 0,3\rrbracket
:

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]=\beta_{0},\hskip 56.9055pt\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]\right)^{\prime}=\theta\beta_{1},\hskip 56.9055pt\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]\right)^{\prime\prime}=\theta^{\prime}\beta_{1}+\theta^{2}\beta_{2},

and

\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]\right)^{(3)}=\theta^{\prime\prime}\beta_{1}+\left(\theta\theta^{\prime}+(\theta^{2})^{\prime}\right)\beta_{2}+\theta^{3}\beta_{3}.

With the choice of potential
V(x)=x^{m}
with
m
even, choosing a bounded function
f
with bounded derivatives at all orders and integrating by parts, it holds that for all
k\geq 0
, there exists
\gamma_{0}^{(k)},\dots,\gamma_{k}^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R},

|\beta_{k}(x)|\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\dfrac{\mathcal{O}^{k}[g](x)}{x^{m-1}}\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\dfrac{1}{x^{m-1}}\sum_{j=0}^{k}g^{(j)}(x)\left(\dfrac{\gamma_{j}^{(k)}}{x^{km-j}}+\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{o}\left(\dfrac{1}{x^{km-j}}\right)\right).

When
V(x)=\cosh(\alpha x)
, by the same computation, we get for different
\gamma_{j}^{(k)}

|\beta_{k}(x)|\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}e^{-\alpha|x|}\sum_{j=0}^{k}\gamma_{j}^{(k)}g^{(j)}(x)\left(e^{-k\alpha|x|}+\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{o}\left(e^{-k\alpha|x|}\right)\right).

4.4
Controls on the inverse of the master operator

Since we are going to use the polynomials
P_{i}^{k}
and
Q_{i}^{k}
defined previously in Theorem
4.6
and
4.10
, a lot in our estimates on
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}
, we first need the following lemma which gives the asymptotics of these polynomials when
|x|\rightarrow\infty
. With
\boxed{\alpha\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\dfrac{\rho_{V}}{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}=\theta^{-1}}
, the following result holds.

Lemma 4.12
.

For all
k\geq 1
, for all
j\in\llbracket 0,k\rrbracket

(i)

P_{i}^{k}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i)}\right)(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-k}\right)
,

(ii)

P_{i}^{k}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i)}\right)^{\prime}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-k}\right),

(iii)

Q_{i}^{k}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{k-i}\right)
.

Proof
.

For
i)
, by the Faà di Bruno’s formula, for all
n\geq 0
,

\alpha^{(n)}=\left(\dfrac{-1}{V^{\prime}+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]}\right)^{(n)}=-\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}\dfrac{(-1)^{|\lambda|}|\lambda|!}{\left(V^{\prime}+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]\right)^{|\lambda|+1}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\dfrac{\left(V^{(j+1)}+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]^{(j)}\right)^{\lambda_{j}}}{\lambda_{j}!(j!)^{\lambda_{j}}}.

where the sum is over
\underline{\lambda}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{n}j\lambda_{j}=n
. From assumption
v)
and Lemma
A.2
, we see that

|\alpha^{(n)}(x)|\leq\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}C_{\lambda}\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-1}\right)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-1}\right).

Hence
P_{j}^{k}
, as a homogeneous polynomial in
\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)
of degree
k
, is a
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-k}\right)
.

For the point
ii)
, one has to notice that for each monomial

A_{n}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\alpha^{(j)}\right)^{k_{j}}\right]^{\prime}=\sum_{l=1}^{n}k_{l}\alpha^{(l+1)}\left(\alpha^{(l)}\right)^{k_{l}-1}\prod_{j\neq l}^{n}\left(\alpha^{(j)}\right)^{k_{j}}.

But, we have proven that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha^{(j)}=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-1}\right)
, so by denoting
k\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=\deg(A_{n})
,
A_{n}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-k}\right)
. Therefore, any homogeneous polynomial of degree
k
such as
P_{i}^{k}
in the variables
\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i)}\right)
is a
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-k}\right)
.

Finally for the point
iii)
, it is clear that for all
j\geq 0
,

\theta^{(j)}(x)=V^{(j+1)}(x)-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\right]^{(j)}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)\right).

Thus
Q_{i}^{(k)}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)
as a homogeneous polynomial of degree
k-i
, is a
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{k-i}\right)
.

\square

Now that a convenient expression for
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}
is available thanks to Theorem
4.10
and that the asymptotics polynomial
Q_{i}^{k}
are well-understood thanks to Lemma
4.12
, we can obtain the first control on
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}
for the
H^{n}
-norm.

Theorem 4.13
(
H^{n}
-continuity of
\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
).

Let
n,p\geq 1
, there exists an explicit constant
C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})>0
(depending only on
n
and
V
) such that for all
f\in H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
,

\boxed{\left\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[f]\right\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|f\|_{H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.}

Under the choice of potential
V_{\phi,t}
defined in Theorem
1.3
, for
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the map
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous.

Proof
.

Let
m\leq n
and
(m_{1},\dots,m_{p})\in\mathbb{N}^{p}
be such that
\sum_{i=1}^{p}m_{i}=m
. Let
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
be fixed, we define
h:x_{1}\mapsto\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{p}^{m_{p}}f(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})
and
g=-\mathcal{X}[h]-2P\mathcal{W}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}[h]
. With these notations,

\partial^{\underline{m}}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[f](x_{1},\dots,x_{p})=\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[h\right]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1}).

The idea of the proof is to use the closed forms for
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]^{(n)}
and
\mathcal{O}^{k}
found respectively in Theorem
4.10
and Theorem
4.6
to get the control for
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
. The bound for
|x_{1}|\leq M_{V}
relies on Lemma
A.3
and the fact that
\rho_{V}
is bounded by below on this set.

Control for
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
.
For
|x_{1}|\geq M_{V}
, we can apply Theorem
4.10
and Theorem
4.6
, to get :

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})=\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x_{1})\beta_{m_{1}-i}(x_{1})\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x_{1})\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\mathcal{O}^{m_{1}-i}[g](t)\\
=\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{1}-i}Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x_{1})\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(t)P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)})(t).

(43)

Moreover an integration by parts yields:

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})=\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{1}-i}Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x_{1})\Bigg(-g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(x_{1})\alpha(x_{1})P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)})(x_{1})\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\Bigg[g^{(m_{1}-i-j+1)}(t)\alpha(t)P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)(t)\\
+g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(t)\left[\alpha P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\Bigg]\Bigg).

(44)

From Lemma
4.8
, the functions
P^{i}_{j}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)
that appear above are well-defined on
[-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}

i.e.
they don’t have any singularity on this set. Hence by integrating with respect to
x_{1}
,
\left({{\Xi}^{-1}[h]^{(m_{1})}}\right)^{2}
on
[M_{V},+\infty[
, we get by Jensen’s inequality for a constant
C>0
depending only on
m_{1}

\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\left(\widetilde{{\Xi}^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})\right)^{2}\\
\leq C\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{1}-i}\int\limits_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i)}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\Bigg\{g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(x_{1})^{2}\alpha(x_{1})^{2}P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)})(x_{1})^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)g^{(m_{1}-i-j+1)}(t)\alpha(t)P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)(t)\right]^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(t)\left[\alpha P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}\Bigg\}.

We want to bound this expression by
\|g\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}
, but since
g=-h+2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right]+\mathfrak{c}_{h},

where

\mathfrak{c}_{h}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(y)-2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right](y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(y),

the constant terms will fail to be in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
. We thus have to treat these terms separately. In the previous sum,
g
is differentiated everywhere except in the term
j=m_{1}-i
so this is the only value of
j
where we have to deal with
\mathfrak{c}
. By defining for
\bm{i}=(i_{1},i_{2})
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)\alpha(x)P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(x)

(45)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}_{m,\bm{i}}^{(2)}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{V}(x)}\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t),

(46)

keeping the
V
-dependence implicit in those functions, Jensen’s inequality and inequality (
30
) yields:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{h}^{2}C\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{i}\\
i_{1}+i_{2}=m_{1}\\
0\leq i_{1}\leq m_{1}-1\end{subarray}}\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}

\displaystyle\left[\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}(x_{1})^{2}+\mathfrak{f}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}^{(2)}(x_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{\bm{i}}\max_{1\leq j\leq 2}\max_{m\leq n}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j)}_{m,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\left(\|h\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu_{V})}+\left\|\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right]\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu_{V})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(V)\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\pi^{2}\|\Xi^{-1}[h]\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{V})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C(V)\left(\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\pi^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2}\|h^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{V})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(V)\|\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{p}^{m_{p}}f(.,x_{2},\dots,x_{p})\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

where at the end, the constant
C_{1}(V)
is defined, for a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
, by:

C_{1}(V)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\max_{\bm{i}}\max_{1\leq j\leq 2}\max_{m\leq n}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j)}_{m,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2}\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{3}\right).

(47)

Above, the first integral that appears is well-defined, since by Lemma
4.12
, one can check by assumption
(v)
that
\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}(x)
and
\mathfrak{f}^{(2)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}(x)
behave like
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-2}\right)
which is integrable by assumption
(v)
again.

In the following, we set
\mathfrak{g}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}g-\mathfrak{c}_{h}
. We can now replace
g^{\prime}
by
\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}
since we handled all the terms involving
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, with
C_{2}(V)
defined, for a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
by:

C_{2}(V)=C\max_{\bm{i}}\max_{m\leq n}\left(\Big\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m,\bm{i}}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}+\max_{3\leq j\leq 4}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j)}_{m,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\right),

(48)

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}_{m,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{V}(x)}\Bigg|\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)^{2}\alpha(t)^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)^{2}\Bigg|^{1/2},

(49)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(4)}_{m,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{V}(x)}\Bigg|\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)^{2}\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\Bigg|^{1/2},

(50)

we get, with
|\bm{i}|=i_{1}+i_{2}
:

\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\Bigg\{\mathfrak{g}^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}(x_{1})^{2}\alpha(x_{1})^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(x_{1})^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\mathfrak{g}^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1)}(t)\alpha(t)P_{i_{2}}^{n_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)\right]^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\mathfrak{g}^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}(t)\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}\Bigg\}\\
\leq C_{2}(V)\|\mathfrak{g}\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

Finally, by using that
\mathfrak{g}=-h-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right],
that
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{H}
is an isometry in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and that for all
u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
,
\mathcal{H}[u]^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}[u^{\prime}]
, we obtain for a universal constant
C>0
,

\left\|{\Xi}^{-1}[h]^{(m_{1})}\right\|_{L^{2}([M_{V},+\infty[)}\leq C\max_{i=1,2}C_{i}(V)^{1/2}\Big(\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}\Big).

We now use the form stated in Lemma
A.3
, to conclude that

\left\|{\Xi}_{1}^{-1}[h]^{(m_{1})}\right\|_{L^{2}([M_{V},+\infty[)}\leq C\max_{i=1,2}C_{i}(V)^{1/2}\left(C_{3}(V,n)+1\right)\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}.

where
C_{3}(V,n)
is given by Lemma
A.3
. The exact same bounds holds on
]-\infty,-M_{V}]
. Now relaxing the dependence on
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
and integrating with respect to these variables, we get

\left\|\partial^{\underline{m}}{\Xi}_{1}^{-1}[f]\right\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1})}\leq C\max_{i=1,2}C_{i}(V)^{1/2}\left(C_{3}(V,n)+1\right)\\
\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p-1}}\|\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{p}^{m_{p}}f(.,x_{2},\dots,x_{p})\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{2}\dots\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{p}\right)^{1/2}.

Thus we deduce that for a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
such that

\left\|\Xi_{1}^{-1}[f]\right\|_{H^{n}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1})}\leq C\max_{m_{1}\leq n}\max_{i=1,2}C_{i}(V)^{1/2}\left(C_{3}(V,n)+1\right)\|f\|_{H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

(51)

Control for
|x_{1}|<M_{V}
.

Now, we prove the control on
[-M_{V},M_{V}]\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
, we fix
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:

\int_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\left|\Xi^{-1}\left[h\right]^{(m_{1})}(x)\right|^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\leq\left\|\rho_{V}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\left\|\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}\left[h\right]^{(m_{1})}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\\
\leq C_{3}(V,n)^{2}\left\|\rho_{V}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\left\|h\right\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

where the last inequality comes from Lemma
A.3
. Again relaxing the dependence on
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
and integrating with respect to these variables, we get for a constant
C>0
dependent only on
n
:

\left\|\Xi_{1}^{-1}[f]\right\|_{H^{n}([-M_{V},M_{V}]\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1})}\leq C\max_{m_{1}\leq n}C_{3}(V,n)\left\|\rho_{V}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}\|f\|_{H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

Collecting the last bound and (
51
) leads to the following definition of
C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
for a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
:

C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\left(C_{3}(V,n)+1\right)\left[\left\|\rho_{V}^{-1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}+\max_{i=1,2}C_{i}(V)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right].

(52)

The fact that, upon choosing the potential
V_{\phi,t}
with
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
t\mapsto C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous is shown in Proposition
B.8
.

\square

We now prove the analogous result for the norm
W_{n}^{\infty}
defined in (
13
) and the associated space of functions
W_{n}^{\infty}
defined in (
12
).

Theorem 4.14
(
W_{n}^{\infty}
-continuity of
\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
).

Let
n,p\geq 1
, there exists an explicit constant
C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})>0
(depending only on
n
and
V
), such that for all
f\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
,

\boxed{\big\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[f]\big\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{{}^{p}})}.}

Under the choice of potential
V_{\phi,t}
defined in Theorem
1.3
, for
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the map
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous.

Proof
.

Let
f\in W_{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p})
, let
m\leq n
and
(m_{1},\dots,m_{p})\in\mathbb{N}^{p}
be such that
\sum_{i=1}^{p}m_{i}=m
, let
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
, we set
h:x_{1}\mapsto\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{p}^{m_{p}}f(x_{1},\dots,x_{p})
we know by theorem
4.10
that

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h](x_{1})=\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)g(t),\quad\quad\quad\text{with}\quad g\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\mathcal{X}[h]+2P\mathcal{X}\circ\mathcal{H}\big[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\big].

(53)

The idea of the proof is to consider separatly the case with
m_{1}=0
and
m_{1}\neq 0
. The first case follows from straightforward bounds. For the case
m_{1}\neq 0
and
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
, we rely on the formula (
43
) obtained in the previous proof. We successively use Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
A.2
as well as the isometry property of the Hilbert transform to deduce the desired bound. For the case
m_{1}\neq 0
and
|x_{1}|\leq M_{V}
, we use Leibniz formula in (
41
) to deduce a manageable expression (
61
) which allows to deduce this last bound and conclude the continuity result for
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[f]
.

Case
m_{1}=0
.
For the following, we define

\mathcal{I}_{1}:x\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x)}\left|\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\right|,\hskip 56.9055pt\mathcal{I}_{2}:x\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x)}\left|\displaystyle\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\rho_{V}(t)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\right|^{1/2}.

(54)

By integration by parts, one can see that
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{V}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-1}\right)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
. So for the first and third term, by direct bounds:

\Bigg|\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\left(-h(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(s)\right)\Bigg|\leq 2\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|h\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

For the two last terms, we want to use that
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{H}
is an isometry on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, so we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequa,lity and the fact that
\mathcal{I}_{2}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-1/2}\right)
is bounded on
\mathbb{R}
so that:

\sup_{x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigg|\dfrac{2P}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\mathcal{X}\circ\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right](t)\Bigg|\leq C_{4}(V)\left\|h^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\leq C_{4}(V)\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

with a universal constant
C>0
,

C_{4}(V)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\max_{1\leq i\leq 2}\|\mathcal{I}_{i}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}}.

(55)

Thus, by taking the supremum of
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
, we conclude that for
m_{1}=0
,

\Big\|\partial^{\underline{m}}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[f]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq\left(2\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{\infty}+C_{4}(V)\right)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

(56)

Case
m_{1}\neq 0
,
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
.
For
m_{1}\neq 0
by (
43
), for all
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
, by Lemma
4.8
we have:

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})=\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{1}-i}\dfrac{Q_{i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(a)}\right)(x_{1})}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}g^{(m_{1}-i-j)}(t)P_{j}^{m_{1}-i}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(j)})(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)

where
g\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\mathcal{X}[h]-2P\mathcal{W}\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{L}^{-1}}[h]
.
Furthermore, setting just as before

\mathfrak{c}_{h}=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)-2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right](t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)

and for
\bm{i}=(i_{1},i_{2})
:

\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{V}(x)}\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(t)\Big|\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t,

(57)

we can bound every term in the previous sum, involving
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
,
i.e.
for index
\bm{i}
such that
i_{2}=m_{1}-i_{1}
, with a universal constant
C>0
:

|\mathfrak{c}\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}(x_{1})|\leq C(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}})\cdot\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

We directly bound
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
in the LHS, while to bound
\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}
, we successfully applied Jensen’s inequality, used the isometry property of
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{H}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and used the inequality (
30
). Furthermore, the fact that
\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}
is bounded on
[-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}
comes from Lemma
4.12
. Finally, by setting
\mathfrak{g}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}g-\mathfrak{c}
, it only remains to establish the following bounds:

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}

\displaystyle Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathfrak{g}^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(t)\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\Bigg\{\Bigg\|\dfrac{\Big|Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)\Big|}{\rho_{V}}\int\limits_{.}^{\mathrm{sgn}(.)\infty}\Bigg|\mathcal{H}\left[\left(\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}\right](t)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\times\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(t)\Big|\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\Bigg\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}+\|h\|_{W^{\infty}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\Bigg\{\|\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}\cdot\|h\|_{W^{\infty}_{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle+\|\mathfrak{f}^{(6)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}\left\|\mathcal{H}\left[\left(\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\Bigg\}

with
|\bm{i}|=i_{1}+i_{2}
and

\mathfrak{f}^{(6)}_{m,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\Big|Q_{i_{1}}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)\Big|\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x)}\sqrt{\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{m-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(t)\Big|^{2}\rho_{V}(t)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}.

(58)

For each
\bm{i}
,
\mathfrak{f}^{(6)}_{m,\bm{i}}
is bounded on
[-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}
because of Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
A.2
. By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma
A.3
, we get:

\left\|\mathcal{H}\left[\left(\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\pi\Big\|\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\Big\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\pi C_{5}(V,m_{1})\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

Finally by the same reasonnings as before, we get

\Big|\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathfrak{g}^{(m_{1}-|\bm{i}|)}(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(t)\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\Big|\\
\leq C_{6}(V,m_{1})\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}

where
C_{6}(V)
is defined, for a constant
C>0
depending only on
n
, by:

C_{6}(V)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\max_{1\leq l\leq n}(1+C_{5}(V,l))\max_{5\leq j\leq 6}\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j)}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}.

(59)

Thus, we deduce that for a constant
C>0
depending only on
n
:

\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}\\
\leq C\Big[(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}})\max_{m\leq n}\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}+C_{6}(V)\Big]\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

(60)

Case
m_{1}\neq 0
,
|x|\leq M_{V}
.
Now let
x\in[-M_{V},M_{V}]
, by differentiating
m_{1}
times
(
41
)
, the Leibniz formula ensures that there exists polynomials
R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}
depending on
(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)})
and a polynomial
S^{m_{1}}
of degree
m_{1}-1
depending on
(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})
, whose coefficients are independent of
V
such that

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}(x)=\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x)}{\rho_{V}(x)}\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}g(t)\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x)g^{(i)}(x)-g^{(m_{1}-1)}(x).

(61)

We recall that the function
g
was defined in (
53
). Then, for all,
x\in[-M_{V},M_{V}]
, by the same bounds as before with
C_{7}(V)
defined by

C_{7}(V)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\max_{1\leq l\leq n}\Big(\|S^{l}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(l)})\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}\|\rho_{V}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}\\
+l\max_{0\leq i\leq l-2}\|R_{l-i}^{l}\big(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(l-1-i)}\big)\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}+1\Big),

(62)

we obtain:

\Big|\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathcal{X}[h](t)\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\\
+\sum_{a=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-a}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-a)}\right)(x_{1})\mathcal{X}[h]^{(a)}(x_{1})-\mathcal{X}[h]^{(m_{1}-1)}(x_{1})\Big|\leq 2C_{7}(V)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}

and for a universal constant
C>0
:

2P\Big|\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t+R_{m_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1)}\right)(x_{1})\Big|\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right](t)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)\\
\leq C\|\rho_{V}\|^{1/2}_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}}C_{7}(V)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

It remains to bound the terms involving the Hilbert-transform. For that, we use that for all
\phi\in H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})
and
i\in\llbracket 0,m_{1}-1\rrbracket
, for a universal constant
C>0
:

|\mathcal{H}[\phi]^{(i)}(x)|=\sqrt{\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(i)}](x)^{2}}=\sqrt{\int_{+\infty}^{x}2\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(i)}](t)\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(i+1)}](t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}\leq C\sqrt{\|\phi^{(i)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\|\phi^{(i+1)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}}.

Applying those results for
\phi=\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\in H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})
, Lemma
4.7
allows us to conclude that

\|\mathcal{H}[\phi]\|_{W_{m_{1}-1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\sqrt{2}\pi\|\phi\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R})}.

We conclude by Lemma
A.3
that for a universal constant
C>0
:

\|\mathcal{H}[\phi]\|_{W_{m_{1}-1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\sqrt{2}\pi C_{5}(V,n)\|h\|_{W_{m_{1}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq CC_{5}(V,n)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}

and thus, with
C_{7}(V)
defined in (
62
), we get :

2P\Big|\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right](t)\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x_{1})\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right]^{(i)}(x_{1})-\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right]^{(m_{1}-1)}(x_{1})\Big|\\
\leq CC_{5}(V,n)C_{7}(V)\|h\|_{W_{m_{1}-1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq CC_{5}(V,n)C_{7}(V)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

All the previous bounds yield for a constant
C>0
:

\Big\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(m_{1})}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}\leq C\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|^{1/2}_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}}+C_{5}(V,n)\right)C_{7}(V)\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}.

Upon taking the supremum over
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{p-1}
in
(
Proof
)
, and over
m\leq n
, we obtain the conclusion for the constant
C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
defined for a constant
C>0
depending only on
n
,

C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\Big[(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}})\max_{m\leq n}\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(5)}_{m,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}+C_{6}(V)\\
+\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|^{1/2}_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{L}}+C_{5}(V,n)\right)C_{7}(V)+\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{\infty}+C_{4}(V)\Big].

(63)

The fact that, upon choosing the potential
V_{\phi,t}
with
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
t\mapsto C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous is shown in Proposition
B.8
.

\square

Finally, we define the variable insertion operators which will also be involved in the loop equations.

Definition 4.15
.

If
\phi
is a function in
n
variables, we define the
n
-th variable insertion operator
\Theta^{(i)}
as

\Theta^{(i)}[\phi](\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n-1})=\phi(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{i-1},\xi_{1},\xi_{i},\dots,\xi_{n-1})

(64)

We can deduce from Theorem
4.14
, the following corollary which will be necessary for the analysis of loop equations.

Corollary 4.16
.

Let
n,p\geq 1
,
j\in\llbracket 2,p+1\rrbracket
, for all
f\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})
,

\boxed{\left\|\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[f]\right\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq 2C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}}

where the constant
C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
was introduced in Theorem
4.14
.

Proof
.

Let
f\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})
,
x_{1},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\underline{m}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(m_{1},\dots,m_{p})\in\mathbb{N}^{p}
such that
m\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{p}m_{i}\leq n
.

\partial^{\underline{m}}\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}\left[f\right](x_{1},\dots,x_{p})=\partial_{1}^{m_{1}}\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\left[f\right](x_{1},\dots,x_{j-1},x_{1},x_{j}\dots,x_{p})\\
+\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j}^{m_{1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\left[f\right](x_{1},\dots,x_{j-1},x_{1},x_{j}\dots,x_{p}).

Thus
\left\|\partial^{\underline{m}}\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}\left[f\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq 2\left\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[f]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}\leq 2C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|f\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}
.

\square

The last control that we need is on
\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}}^{-1}
in
H^{n}
-norm. We recall that
\Theta^{(j)}
is defined in Definition
4.15
.

Theorem 4.17
(
H^{n}
-continuity for
\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
).

Let
n,p\geq 1
, there exists an explicit constant
C_{H_{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})>0
(depending only on
n
and
V
), such that for all
f\in H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})
and
j\in\llbracket 2,p+1\rrbracket
,

\boxed{\|\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[f]\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq C_{H_{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|f\|_{H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}.}

Under the choice of potential
V_{\phi,t}
defined in Theorem
1.3
, for
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the map
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{H_{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous.

Proof
.

Let
f\in H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})
,
\underline{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,
\underline{m}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(m_{1},\dots,m_{p})\in\mathbb{N}^{p}
such that
m\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{p}m_{i}\leq n
. We set
h:(x,y)\mapsto\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f(x,x_{2},\dots,x_{j-1},y,x_{j},\dots,x_{p})
and

g(x,y)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-h(x,y)+2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[h(.,y)\right]\right](x)+\mathfrak{c}_{h}(y)

where we have set
\mathfrak{c}_{h}(y)=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(s,y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(s)-2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[h(.,y)\right]\right](s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(s)
. Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\partial^{\underline{m}}\Theta^{(j)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}\left[f\right](x_{1},\dots,x_{p})=\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(.,x_{1})](x_{1})+\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h(.,x_{1})]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1}).

(65)

The idea of the proof is to obtain first a straightforward bound on the first term in (
65
). To bound the second term, we treat the cases
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
and
|x_{1}|<M_{V}
differently. For the first case, we rely on the expression (
44
) obtained above for the
m
-
th
derivative of
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]
. We then apply the controls of Lemma
4.12
and
A.3
to deduce the bound outside of the compact. On the compact
|x_{1}|<M_{V}
, we use again (
61
) and Lemma
A.3
to conclude on the last bound and therefore on Theorem (
4.17
).

First term in (
65
).
The first term in the RHS is easy to control by Theorem
4.10
,

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(.,x_{1})](x_{1})=\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})}\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\Bigg\{-\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(t,x_{1})+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(s,x_{1})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(s)\\
+2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(.,x_{1})\right]\right](t)-2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(.,x_{1})\right]\right](s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(s)\Bigg\}.

The same argument as in the previous controls give that there exists a universal constant
C>0
such that:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial_{2}^{m_{1}}h(.,x_{1})](x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\\
\leq C(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2})\Big[\|\mathcal{I}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\Big]\cdot\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

(66)

where
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
have been defined in (
54
).

Bound for
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
.
We now deal with the second term in (
65
). By (
44
), we have for a constant
C>0
depending only on
m_{1}
:

\displaystyle\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}

\displaystyle\Xi^{-1}[h(.,x_{1})]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\leq C\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{[\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}g(x_{1},x_{1})]^{2}\alpha(x_{1})^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(x_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}g(t,x_{1})\alpha(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}g(t,x_{1})\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}\Bigg\}.

(67)

Above, we used the notation
\bm{i}=(i_{1},i_{2})
and
|\bm{i}|=i_{1}+i_{2}
. We first deal with the presence of
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
in the sum. This term only arises in the sum when
i_{2}=m_{1}-i_{1}
. By using the functions
\mathfrak{f}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}^{(k)}
for
k\in\{1,2\}
defined in (
45
), we can bound these terms, for all
i_{1}\in\llbracket 0,m_{1}-1\rrbracket
, by using a universal constant
C>0
:

\max_{\bm{i}}\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\mathfrak{c}_{h}(x_{1})^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{2}\mathfrak{f}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}^{(k)}(x_{1})^{2}\\
\leq C\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{f}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}^{(k)}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(t,x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\left[h(.,x_{1})\right]\right](t)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)\right)\\
\leq C\max_{\bm{i}}\max_{k\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathfrak{f}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}^{(k)}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}(\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{3}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2})\cdot\|h\|^{2}_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Since we handled all the therms involving
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
, it just remains to bound (
67
) with the substitution
g(x,y)\rightsquigarrow\mathfrak{g}(x,y)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}g(x,y)-\mathfrak{c}_{h}(y)
, namely:

\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\Bigg\{\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}\alpha(x_{1})^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})})(x_{1})^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\alpha(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)\right]^{2}\\
+\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}\Bigg\}.

For the first term, we use the fact that for fixed
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t\mapsto\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(t,x)
goes to zero at infinity as an element of
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we get:

|\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})|=\sqrt{\int^{x_{1}}_{+\infty}2\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathfrak{g}(.,x_{1})\|_{H^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}(\mathbb{R})}.

Furthermore, for all
|x_{1}|>M_{V}
and all
\bm{i}
,
\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}
is bounded since it is continuous and a
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}\left(V^{\prime}(x)^{-2}\right)
by Lemma
4.12
. We conclude, by Lemma
A.3
that, with
\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}
being given in (
45
), there exists a constant
C
depending only on
n
such that:

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}=0}^{m_{1}-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}

\displaystyle\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}^{2}\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}(x_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\int_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\|\mathfrak{g}(.,x_{1})\|_{H^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\|\mathfrak{g}\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}C_{3}(V,n)\right]\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\cdot\|\partial_{3}^{m_{3}}f(.,z)\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

It just remains to bound

\int\limits_{M_{V}}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})^{2}}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\Bigg\{\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\alpha(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)\right]^{2}\\
+\left[\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\left[\alpha P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(t)\right]^{2}\Bigg\}.

For the first term, we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Lemma
4.12
and the function
\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}
defined in (
49
) to get for a universal constant
C>0
:

\displaystyle\dfrac{|Q_{i_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(i_{1})}\right)(x_{1})|^{2}}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\rho_{V}(t)\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-|\bm{i}|+1}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\alpha(t)P_{i_{2}}^{m_{1}-i_{1}}\left(\alpha,\dots,\alpha^{(i_{2})}\right)(t)\Bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\|\mathfrak{g}(.,x_{1})\|_{H^{n_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(3)}_{m_{1},\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}C_{3}(V,n)\right)

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f(.,x_{2},\dots,x_{j-1},x_{1},x_{j},\dots,x_{p})\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

We proceed in the exact same way for the second term and do the same thing on
]-\infty,-M_{V}]
, for every term we dealt with. Finally, by integrating with respect to
x_{1}
, collecting all the terms and then integrating over
x_{1}\in[-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}
and over
x_{2},\dots,x_{p}\in\mathbb{R}
, we get

\left\|\Theta^{(j)}\Big[\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial_{2}^{m_{2}}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f]^{(m_{1})}\Big]\right\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1})}\leq C_{9}(V)\cdot\|f\|_{H^{n+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}

with
C_{9}(V)
defined, for a constant
C>0
depending only on
n
, by:

C_{9}(V)^{2}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C(n)\max_{m\leq n}\max_{j\in\{1,3\}}\max_{\bm{i}}\|\mathfrak{f}_{m,\bm{i}}^{(j)}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c})}^{2}\\
\times\left(\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{3}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2}+1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}C_{3}(V,n)\right).

(68)

Bound for
|x_{1}|<M_{V}
.
It just remains to bound

\displaystyle\int_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h(.,x_{1})]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}
. For that, we use (
61
), to get for a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h(.,x_{1})]^{(m_{1})}(x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\\
\leq C\displaystyle\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\Bigg\{\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})^{2}}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left(\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}g(t,x_{1})\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x_{1})^{2}[\partial_{1}^{i}g(x_{1},x_{1})]^{2}+[\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-1}g(x_{1},x_{1})]^{2}\Bigg\}.

By the same procedure as before, we first deal with
\mathfrak{c}_{h}
defined at the beginning of the proof, this yields, with
\mathcal{I}_{i}
defined in (
54
) and
C>0
depending only on
n
:

\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\mathfrak{c}_{h}(x_{1})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\Bigg[\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})^{2}}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left(\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}+R_{m_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\Bigg]\\
\leq C\|h\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2}\right)\cdot\Big\{\|\mathcal{I}_{1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big\|S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\\
+\Big\|R_{m_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1)}\right)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\Big\}.

Hence as before, we can replace
g
by
\mathfrak{g}
and conclude with the last bounds:

\displaystyle\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\dfrac{S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})(x_{1})^{2}}{\rho_{V}(x_{1})^{2}}\left(\int\limits_{x_{1}}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x_{1})\infty}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\rho_{V}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\\
\leq\Big\|\mathcal{I}_{2}S^{m_{1}}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1})})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\cdot\|\mathfrak{g}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2},

and by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, with a constant
C>0
only depending on
n
:

\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\partial_{1}^{i}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}+\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-1}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}\Big)\\
\leq\Bigg\{1+\max_{i\in\llbracket 0,m_{1}-2\rrbracket}\Big\|R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\Bigg\}\\
\times\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-1}\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\int\limits_{+\infty}^{x_{1}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\partial_{1}^{i+1}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\partial_{1}^{i}\mathfrak{g}(t,x_{1})\\
\leq C\Bigg\{1+\max_{i\in\llbracket 0,m_{1}-2\rrbracket}\Big\|R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\Bigg\}\cdot\|\mathfrak{g}\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Moreover, by Lemma
A.3
, we have for
C>0
a universal constant:

\|\mathfrak{g}\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}+2P\pi\left\|\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}_{1}}[h]\right\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C\left[1+C_{3}(V,n)\right]\cdot\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

We can then conclude that, for
C>0
depending only on
n
:

\int\limits_{-M_{V}}^{M_{V}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x_{1}\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{m_{1}-2}R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)(x_{1})^{2}\partial_{1}^{i}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}+\partial_{1}^{m_{1}-1}\mathfrak{g}(x_{1},x_{1})^{2}\Big)\\
\leq C\left[1+C_{3}(V,n)\right]\Bigg\{1+\max_{i\in\llbracket 0,m_{1}-2\rrbracket}\Big\|R_{m_{1}-i}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m_{1}-1-i)}\right)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\Bigg\}\cdot\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}.

Thus by integrating with respect to
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we get,

\left\|\Theta^{(j)}\Big[\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial^{m_{2}}_{2}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f]^{(m_{1})}\Big]\right\|_{L^{2}([-M_{V},M_{V}]\times\mathbb{R}^{p-1})}\leq C_{10}(V,n)\cdot\|\partial_{3}^{m_{3}}f\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}

with
C_{10}(V)>0
defined, for a constant
C>0
depending only on
n
, by:

C_{10}(V)^{2}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\max_{m\leq n}\Bigg\{\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2}\right)\\
\times\Big\{\left[1+C_{3}(V,n)\right]^{2}\Bigg(\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathcal{I}_{i}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big\|S^{m}(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m)})\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\\
+1+\max_{i\in\llbracket 0,m-2\rrbracket}\Big\|R_{m-i}^{m}\left(\theta,\dots,\theta^{(m-1-i)}\right)\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([-M_{V},M_{V}])}^{2}\Bigg)\Bigg\}.

(69)

Collecting the bounds on the
L^{2}
-norms of
\Theta^{(j)}\Big[\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial^{m_{2}}_{2}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f]^{(m_{1})}\Big]
on
[-M_{V},M_{V}]^{c}\times\mathbb{R}
and
[-M_{V},M_{V}]\times\mathbb{R}
, we obtain:

\left\|\Theta^{(j)}\Big[\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\partial^{m_{2}}_{2}\dots\partial_{j-1}^{m_{j-1}}\partial_{j+1}^{m_{j}}\dots\partial_{p+1}^{m_{p}}f]^{(m_{1})}\Big]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{p})}\leq 2\max_{i\in\{9,10\}}C_{i}(V)\cdot\|h\|_{H^{m_{1}+1}(\mathbb{R}^{p+1})}.

By combining the bound above together with (
66
) and taking the supremum over
m\leq n
, we get the desired conclusion
with,
C_{9}
and
C_{10}
being given in (
68
) and (
69
),
\mathcal{I}_{i}
being given in (
54
);

C_{H_{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C\max_{i\in\{9,10\}}C_{i}(V)\\
+(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}C_{\mathcal{L}}^{2})\left(1+\|\rho_{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\right)\max_{i\in\{1,2\}}\|\mathcal{I}_{i}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

(70)

Noticing that this constant does not depend on
j
yields the conclusion. The fact that, upon choosing the potential
V_{\phi,t}
with
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
t\mapsto C_{H_{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
is continuous is shown in Proposition
B.8
.

\square

5
Asymptotic expansion of the linear statistics

In this section, we prove the loop equations for general functions, using the continuity results proven in Sections
3
and
4
. We then prove Theorem
1.2
by using the
a priori
bound proven in Proposition
2.4
and again the continuity results proven in Sections
3
and
4
.

5.1
Loop equations for general functions

We now have all the needed ingredients to state the loop equations, we recall the definition of a linear statistic was defined in (
8
). We follow the approach of
[
15
, Prop 3.2.3]
and adapt it to our setting. Since the equilibium measure and thus the master operator are different in the high-temperature regime (compared to the fixed-temperature regime), the standard arguments of recentring and extension to general functions by density must be adapted.

Theorem 5.1
(Loop equations).

The level
1
loop equation holds for all
\psi_{1}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R})
and takes the form:

\Braket{\psi_{1}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}.

(71)

For all
\psi_{n}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, the level
n>1
loop equations reads:

\Braket{\psi_{n}}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n}]}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n}]}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n}]}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\\
-\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=2}^{n}\Bigg(\Braket{\Theta^{(i)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{i}\psi_{n}\big]}_{\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}+\Braket{\Theta^{(i)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{i}\psi_{n}\big]}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n-2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\Bigg).

(72)

Proof
.

Let
\big(\psi^{(i)}\big)_{i=1}^{n+1}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R})^{n+1}
and set
\phi^{(1)}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi^{(1)}]

, set
V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}(\lambda)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}V(\lambda)+\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\epsilon_{i}\psi^{(i)}(\lambda_{i})
and then define

p_{N}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}(\underline{\lambda})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\dfrac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}]}\cdot\prod_{i<j}^{N}|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}|^{\frac{2P}{N}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}\cdot e^{-V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}(\lambda_{i})}

We then define
G_{t}(\mu)=\mu+t\phi^{(1)}(\mu)
. We claim that
\partial_{1}\phi^{(1)}
is bounded. Indeed because
\psi^{(1)}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R})
and Theorem
4.13
,
\phi^{(1)}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R})
so in particular
\partial_{1}\phi^{(1)}
is bounded.

As a consequence, for
t
small enough
G_{t}
is a diffeomorphism over
\mathbb{R}
. By a change of variable
\lambda_{i}=G_{t}(\mu_{i})
, we obtain :

1=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}p_{N}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{N})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\underline{\lambda}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}p_{N}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}\Big(G_{t}(\mu_{1},\dots,G_{t}(\mu_{N})\Big)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{N}G_{t}^{\prime}(\mu_{i})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\underline{\mu}.

Making an asymptotic expansion up to the first order in
t
of the right-hand side of this equality yields:

1=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\lambda\cdot p_{N}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}(\underline{\lambda})\cdot\Bigg\{1+t\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{1}\phi^{(1)}(\lambda_{i})\Bigg\}\cdot\Bigg\{1+t\dfrac{P}{N}\sum_{i\neq j}\dfrac{\phi^{(1)}(\lambda_{i})-\phi^{(1)}(\lambda_{j})}{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\Bigg\}\\
\cdot\Bigg\{1-t\sum_{i=1}^{N}V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}(\lambda_{i})\phi^{(1)}(\lambda_{i})\Bigg\}+O(t^{2}).

Identifying the terms linear in
t
leads to:

0=-N\Braket{V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\phi^{(1)}}_{\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+PN\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\mu_{N}\otimes\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+(N-P)\Braket{\partial_{1}\phi^{(1)}}_{\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}

where we used, for any (signed) measure
\nu
, the following notation

\Braket{f}_{\nu}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\nu(x)\right)\cdot p_{N}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}(\underline{\lambda})\cdot\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\underline{\lambda}.

By definition of
V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}
, we get:

\Braket{V^{\prime}\phi^{(1)}}_{\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_{i}\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\mu_{N}\otimes\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-\left(1-\dfrac{P}{N}\right)\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}=0.

It becomes, after recentring the empirical measures against
\mu_{V}
we obtain:

\Braket{V^{\prime}\phi^{(1)}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{V^{\prime}\phi^{(1)}}_{\mu_{V}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-2P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\mu_{V}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\mu_{V}\otimes\mu_{V}}\\
-\Big(1-\dfrac{P}{N}\Big)\left(\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}\right)+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_{i}\Big(\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}\Big)=0.

Using the following identities true for all signed measure
\nu
,

\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\nu\otimes\mu_{V}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}=-\Braket{\phi^{(1)}\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]}_{\nu}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(1)}\rho_{V}]}_{\nu}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})};\hskip 28.45274pt\Braket{\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}{\rho_{V}}\phi^{(1)}}_{\mu_{V}}=-\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}},

and

V^{\prime}+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]=-\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}{\rho_{V}},\hskip 42.67912pt\Braket{\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(1)}\rho_{V}]}_{\mu_{V}}=-\Braket{\phi^{(1)}\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]}_{\mu_{V}},

we get:

-\Braket{\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}{\rho_{V}}\phi^{(1)}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-2P\Braket{\mathcal{H}[\phi^{(1)}\rho_{V}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}\\
+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_{i}\Big(\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}\Big)=0.

which leads to:

\Braket{\Xi[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}\\
+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\varepsilon_{i}\Big(\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{(\underline{\varepsilon})}+\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}\Big)

(73)

Taking
\varepsilon_{i}
go to 0 for all
i
,
\phi^{(1)}=\Xi^{-1}[\psi]
leads to:

\Braket{\Xi[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}.

And by definition of
\phi^{(1)}
and the fact that
\Delta\mu_{N}
is a measure zero mass, we obtain:

\Braket{\psi^{(1)}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi^{(1)}]^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi^{(1)}]^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi^{(1)}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}.

Hence we obtain (
71
).

Furthermore, in (
73
) multiplying by
\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V_{\underline{\varepsilon}}]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V]}
, applying
\partial_{\varepsilon_{2}}\dots\partial_{\varepsilon_{n+1}}
and evaluate at
\varepsilon_{i}=0
leads to

(-N)^{n}\Braket{\Xi[\phi^{(1)}](\xi_{1})\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}\psi^{(i)}(\xi_{i})}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=(-N)^{n}\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}(\xi_{1})\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}\psi^{(i)}(\xi_{i})}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\\
+(-N)^{n}\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{(\phi^{(1)})^{\prime}(\xi_{1})\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}\psi^{(i)}(\xi_{i})}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-P(-N)^{n}\Braket{\mathcal{D}[\phi^{(1)}](\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\prod_{i=2}^{n+1}\psi^{(i)}(\xi_{i+1})}_{\overset{n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\\
+(-N)^{n-1}\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\Bigg(\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\xi_{1})(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}(\xi_{1})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=2\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n+1}\psi^{(j)}(\xi_{j})}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\hskip-28.45274pt+\Braket{\phi^{(1)}(\xi_{1})(\psi^{(i)})^{\prime}(\xi_{1})\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=2\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n+1}\psi^{(j)}(\xi_{j})}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\hskip-28.45274pt\Bigg)

(74)

Using again that
\phi^{(1)}=\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi^{(1)}]
and that
\Delta\mu_{N}
has zero mass, dividing by
(-N)^{n}
and defining
\psi_{n+1}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n+1})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\psi^{(i)}(\xi_{i})
and using the operators defined earlier leads to

\Braket{\psi_{n+1}}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n+1}]}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n+1}]}_{\overset{n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{n+1}]}_{\overset{n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\\
-\dfrac{1}{N}\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}\Bigg(\Braket{\Theta^{(i)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{i}\psi_{n+1}\big]}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}+\Braket{\Theta^{(i)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{i}\psi_{n+1}\big]}_{\mu_{V}\overset{n-1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}\Bigg).

(75)

It then not hard to see that, since
\bigotimes^{n+1}H^{3}(\mathbb{R})
is dense in
H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
for
\|.\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})}
, because of Theorem
3.3
,
4.13
and
4.17
, (
75
) remains valid for all
\psi_{n+1}\in H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
. This establishes (
72
).

\square

5.2
Asymptotic expansion of linear statistics

The a priori bound on linear statistics of Proposition
2.4
, provides the main ingredient for obtaining the existence of their large-
N
asymptotic expansion in powers of
N^{-1}
up to any order using the loop equations Theorem
5.1
.

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\psi_{k}\in\displaystyle H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
, then for all integer
K
, there exists a sequence
(d_{i}^{(k)})_{i\geq\lceil k/2\rceil}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
(depending on
V
) such that

\boxed{\Braket{\psi_{k}}_{\overset{k}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\sum_{i=\lceil k/2\rceil}^{K}\dfrac{d_{i}^{(k)}(\psi_{k})}{N^{i}}+O\left(N^{-(K+1)}\right)}

with

d_{1}^{(1)}[\psi_{1}]=P\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+P\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\left[\partial_{2}\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]\right]}_{\mu_{V}}.

Furthermore, there exists a sequence of integers
\left(m_{K}\right)>0
(depending on
K
and
k
), increasing in
K
, such that for all
k\geq 1
and
K\geq 0
, all
\psi_{k}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
,

\Bigg|\Braket{\psi_{k}}_{\overset{k}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-\sum_{i=\lceil k/2\rceil}^{K}\dfrac{d_{i}^{(k)}(\psi_{k})}{N^{i}}\Bigg|\leq\dfrac{C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V)}{N^{K+1}}\mathcal{N}_{m_{K}}(\psi_{k}).

(76)

Above
\mathcal{N}_{m}(\psi_{k})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\max\left(\|\psi_{k}\|_{W_{m}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})},\|\psi_{k}\|_{H^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{k})}\right)
, while
C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V)>0
is a constant (depending on
V
,
K
and
k
). Finally, under the choice of potential
V_{\phi,t}
defined in Theorem
1.3
, for
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the map
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V_{\phi,t})
is continuous.

Proof
.

Using the first loop equation given in Theorem
5.1
, we get:

\Braket{\psi_{1}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}.

(77)

where we recall that
\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}=\Xi^{-1}\circ\mathcal{X}
defined in Definition
4.9
.
The idea is to verify the hypotheses of Theorems
2.4
for each function involved in the Dyson-Schwinger equations. By Proposition
3.5
and Theorem
3.3
,
4.13
,
4.14
,
4.16
and
4.17
and the fact that
\psi_{k}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
, we’re ensured that all the norms are finite and that a
n
-linear statistic will be a
O\left(N^{-\frac{n}{2}(1-\varepsilon)}\right)
where
\varepsilon>0
is fixed but can be chosen arbitrarly small.

We show by induction on
K
that there exists an asymptotic expansion up to
o(N^{-K})
for any function
\psi_{k}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
for all
k\leq 2K
.

For
K=1
, since the first term in the RHS of (
77
) clearly contributes to the asymptotic expansion of
\Braket{\psi_{1}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}
up to
o(N^{-1})
, we focus on the two other terms. In (
77
), the second term is clearly a
o(N^{-1})
since by Proposition
2.4
, Theorem
4.13
and Theorem
4.14
, there exists
C>0
(depending only on
\varepsilon>0
)

|\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}|\leq Ce^{K_{V}}N^{-(1-\varepsilon)/2}\left(\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}\|_{H^{1/2}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}\|_{W^{\infty}_{1}(\mathbb{R})}\right)\\
\leq Ce^{K_{V}}N^{-(1-\varepsilon)/2}\left(C_{H^{2}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})+C_{W_{2}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\right)\mathcal{N}_{3}(\psi_{1}).

To obtain the expansion of the
2
-linear statistic up to
o(N^{-1})
, we will need to use the loop equation at level 2 with
\psi_{2}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi]
. Let
\psi_{2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
be arbitrary for now. The level
2
equation reads:

\Braket{\psi_{2}}_{\overset{2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2}]}_{\mu_{V}\bigotimes\Delta\mu_{N}}+\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2}]}_{\overset{2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-\dfrac{1}{N}\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{2}\psi_{2}\big]}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}\\
-P\Braket{\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2}]}_{\overset{3}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-\dfrac{1}{N}\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{2}\psi_{2}\big]}_{\mu_{V}}.

(78)

The first term is a
o(N^{-1})
as a 1-linear statistic
\Braket{\psi}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}
where
\psi(x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2}](x,y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(y)
. This function is indeed in
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
because Theorem
4.13
gives:

\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\leq\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}\|\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}[}\psi_{2}]\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\leq\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}C_{H^{2}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})^{2}\cdot\|\psi_{2}\|_{H^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

and in
W_{1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
by Theorem
4.14

\|\psi\|_{W_{1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\|\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}[}\psi_{2}]\|_{W_{1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq C_{W_{2}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\cdot\|\psi_{2}\|_{W_{3}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}.

Thus by the a priori bound Proposition
2.4
, we get for
C>0
(depending only on
\varepsilon
)

\Big|\dfrac{P}{N}\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2}]}_{\mu_{V}\bigotimes\Delta\mu_{N}}\Big|\leq\dfrac{PCe^{K_{V}}}{N^{1+(1-\varepsilon)/2}}\left[C_{W_{2}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})+\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}C_{H^{2}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\right]\mathcal{N}_{3}(\psi_{2}).

(79)

The following two terms in (
78
) are also a
o(N^{-1})
by the same reasons as before. By Proposition
2.4
, the
3
-linear statistics is a
o(N^{-1})
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough. Hence, we obtain the expansion:

\Braket{\psi}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{d_{1}^{(1)}(\psi_{1})}{N}+o(N^{-1})\quad\quad\quad\text{ and }\quad\quad\quad\Braket{\psi_{2}}_{\Delta\mu_{N}\otimes\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{d_{1}^{(2)}(\psi_{2})}{N}+o(N^{-1})

where

d_{1}^{(1)}(\psi_{1})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}P\Braket{\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]^{\prime}}_{\mu_{V}}+P\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\left[\partial_{2}\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[\psi_{1}]\right]}_{\mu_{V}}

and

d_{1}^{(2)}(\psi_{2})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\big[\partial_{2}\psi_{2}\big]}_{\mu_{V}}.

More generally, suppose the desired expansion for
\Braket{\psi_{k}}_{\overset{k}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}
holds up to
o(N^{-n})
for all
k\in\llbracket 0,2n\rrbracket
and for any function
\psi_{k}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{k})
. Additionnaly, suppose that (
76
) is true for all
k\in\llbracket 1,K-2\rrbracket
. Then, taking a general function
\psi_{2n+2}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2n+2})
, the
(2n+2)
-
th
equation involves the
(2n+3)
-linear statistic
\Braket{\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\Xi_{1}^{-1}[\psi_{2n+2}]}_{\overset{2n+3}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}
(see (
72
)). By Proposition
2.4
, it is of size
o\left(N^{-(n+1)}\right)
for
\varepsilon
small enough. The other terms will be either,
(2n+1)
-linear statistics with a
N^{-1}
prefactor and therefore behave like
o(N^{-(n+1)})
for
\varepsilon
small enough, or a
2n
-linear statistics with a prefactor
N^{-1}
. For the latter, by hypothesis, we know the asymptotic expansion up to
o(N^{-n})
, thus with the prefactor
N^{-1}
, we deduce the following expansion for
\braket{\psi_{2n+2}}_{\overset{2n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}
:

\braket{\psi_{2n+2}}_{\overset{2n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{d_{n+1}^{(2n+2)}(\psi_{2n+2})}{N^{n+1}}+o\left(N^{-(n+1)}\right).

We now deduce from the equation above, the expansion of
\braket{\psi_{2n+1}}_{\overset{2n+1}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}
for a general
\psi_{2n+1}
belonging to
H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2n+1})
. In the
(2n+1)
-th
loop equation (
72
), the
(2n+2)
-linear statistic yields a non-trivial term of order
N^{-(n+1)}

i.e.
:

\Braket{\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\Xi_{1}^{-1}[\psi_{2n+1}]}_{\overset{2n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}=\dfrac{d_{n+1}^{(2n+2)}\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}[\psi_{2n+1}]\right)}{N^{n+1}}+o\left(N^{-(n+1)}\right).

Again, the
(2n+1)
-linear statistics with a prefactor
N^{-1}
is a
o(N^{-(n+1)})
. Finally for the
2n
and
(2n-1)
linear statistics with the prefactor
N^{-1}
appearing in the
(2n+1)
-th
loop equation, their known expansion up to
o(N^{-(n+1)})
(by hypothesis) leads to the expansion of
\braket{\psi_{2n+2}}_{\overset{2n+2}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}
up to
o(N^{-(n+1)})
.

To conclude on the expansion of the
2n
-linear statisticsup to
o(N^{-(n+1)})
, notice that for each term appearing in the
(2n)
-
th
equation each term will either be a
(2n+1)
-linear statistics for which we know the expansion up to
o(N^{-(n+1)})
, or a linear statistic for which we know, by hypothesis, the asymptotic expansion up to
o(N^{-n})
(of order
2n
,
2n-1
or
2n-2
), preceded by a factor
N^{-1}
. We can therefore conclude on the existence of the expansion of the
2n
-linear statistics up to
o(N^{-(n+1)})
. Then applying the same arguments for
2n-1
,…
1
-linear statistics allows us to conclude that the induction step is established.

Finally, to conclude on (
76
), one must simply notice that for all
n\geq 1
and
\psi_{n}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, for all
K\geq\lceil n/2\rceil
,

\Braket{\psi_{n}}_{\overset{n}{\bigotimes}\Delta\mu_{N}}-\sum_{i=\lceil n/2\rceil}^{K}\dfrac{d_{i}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})}{N^{i}}=\dfrac{d_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})}{N^{K+1}}+\mathfrak{R}_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ and }\hskip 14.22636pt\mathsf{R}_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})=o(N^{-(K+1)}).

Above, the remainder
\mathsf{R}_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})
contains all the negligible statistics involving the operators
\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
,
\mathcal{D}_{1}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
and
\Theta^{(a)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}
. Thus just as in (
79
), by using continuity of the different operators involved in each of the statistics, there exists
m>0
(depending only on
K
and
n
), a polynomial
\mathsf{Q}_{K}^{(1)}
(whose coefficients only depend on
K
and
n
) in
e^{K_{V}}
,
\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}
,
C_{H^{m}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
,
C_{W_{m}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
and
C_{H^{m}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
with coefficients independent of
V
and a constant
C>0
(depending only on
K
and
n
) such that:

\bigg|\mathsf{R}_{K+1}^{(n)}[\psi_{n}]\bigg|\leq\dfrac{C}{N^{K+1}}\mathsf{Q}^{(1)}_{K}\cdot\mathcal{N}_{m}(\psi_{n}).

To bound,
d_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})
and extract the
V
-dependence, one just notices that it is a sum of linear statistics, involving as before the previous operators. By contintuity of these operators, there exists a polynomial
\mathsf{Q}_{K}^{(2)}
(whose coefficients are independent of
V
) in the previous operator norms and
\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}
such that, choosing
m>0
and
C>0
(a constant independent of
V
and
\psi_{n}
) big enough, such that:

\bigg|d_{K+1}^{(n)}(\psi_{n})\bigg|\leq C\mathsf{Q}^{(2)}_{K}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{(n)}(\psi_{n}).

Thus setting
c=C\left[\mathsf{Q}^{(1)}_{K}+\mathsf{Q}^{(2)}_{K}\right]
allows us to conclude about
76
. The fact that
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V_{\phi,t})
is continuous follows from the fact it is a polynomial in building blocks which are continuous as it is shown in Appendix
B
, Lemma
B.10
, Proposition
B.8
.

\square

6
Parameter continuity of the equilibrium measure

We want to conclude about the asymptotic expansion of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G,\phi}\right]
for a smooth
\phi,
by inserting the asymptotic expansion of the linear statistics of Theorem
5.2
in Lemma
7.3
. In order to make that step rigorous, it is necessary to prove that all the linear statistics integrated with respect to the probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{N}^{V_{G,\phi,t}}
with
t\in[0,1]
and then
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
, yield a definite and finite integral. Since all the quantities depend on
t
through the equilibrium measure
\mu_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
, we first prove a continuity result for
t\mapsto\rho_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
. The result that we are going to prove does not depend on the specificity of the quadratic potential so in the following, we set
V_{\phi,t}:x\mapsto V(x)+t\phi(x)
with
V
satisfying the assumptions
1.1
.

While from the measure point of view, it is easy to show that
t\mapsto\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}
is continuous for the weak topology of measures, it is not sufficient to deduce the continuity of the quantities involved in our problem. Indeed, in the controls we showed in Section
4
, quantities like
L^{\infty}
-norm of derivatives of
\rho_{V_{t}}
and
C_{\mathcal{L}}
arose. To prove the integrability of these quantities, it is necessary to show that
\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\|_{W_{i}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0
for all
t_{0}\in[0,1]
.

6.1
Setting for Banach fixed-point theorem

Let
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and let
t_{0}\in[0,1]
, we define the function
u_{t}
by

\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}=(1+\delta tu_{t})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\;\;\;\;\textit{ i.e. }\;\;\;\;u_{t}=\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}{\delta t}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}

(80)

where
t\neq t_{0}
and
\delta t\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}t-t_{0}
. We will show, by Banach fixed-point theorem, that
x\mapsto u_{t}(x)\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, by Lemma
2.1
, is the unique fixed-point of a
t
continuous operator. This will allow us to deduce that
t\mapsto u_{t}
is continuous for the
W^{\infty}_{k}
-norm for all
k\geq 0
. The continuity of
t\mapsto\rho_{V_{t}}\in W^{\infty}_{k}(\mathbb{R})
will then follow.

In order to construct the operator of interest, we start with the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
t,t_{0}\in[0,1]
,

\lambda_{V_{\phi,t}}=\lambda_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}+\delta t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)-2P\delta t\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log|x-y|u_{t}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\log\big(1+\delta tu_{t}(x)\big)-\delta tu_{t}(x)\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x).

(81)

Here
\lambda_{V_{\phi,t}}
denotes the constant appearing in (
5
) with potential
V_{\phi,t}
.

Proof
.

We integrate with respect to
\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
(
5
) to get

\lambda_{V_{\phi,t}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}V_{\phi,t_{0}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)+\delta t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x).

After using the fact that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}u_{t}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)=0
, that
\rho_{V_{\phi,,t}}=(1+\delta tu_{t})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
and the characterization (
5
) of
\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
, this yields the result.

\square

To show that
u_{t}
is a fixed point of a
t
-continuous operator, we need to invert and control the operator
\mathcal{T}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{A}^{-1}
(these operators were inroduced in Definition
4.1
) which appears naturally when comparing
\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}
to
\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

We define the operator
\mathcal{T}
by

\mathcal{T}[v]\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}v-\mathcal{K}[v]
for all
v\in L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
,
where

\mathcal{K}[v](x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}k(x,y)v(y)\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y

and

k(x,y)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\left(\log\dfrac{|x-y|}{1+|x|}-\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\dfrac{|z-y|}{1+|z|}\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}z\right).

The operator
\mathcal{T}:L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
is bijective and for all
n\geq 0
,
\mathcal{T}\left[W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]=W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
Finally, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{\mathcal{T},n}>0
such that for any
v\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

\left\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}[v]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{\mathcal{T},n}\cdot\|v\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

(82)

Proof
.

It was shown in
[
27
, Theorem 6.13]
1
1

1

There is a misprint in the cited Theorem, in (65) it should be
k(x,y)w(y)d\mu_{V}(y)
.
that
\mathcal{T}[v]=-\mathcal{L}\circ\mathcal{A}^{-1}[v]
for all
v\in\mathsf{H}
, where we used that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)=0
. Since
\mathcal{A},\,\mathcal{L}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathsf{H}
are both bijective operators, so is
\mathcal{T}:\mathsf{H}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}
.

The fact that
k
verifies
\|k\|_{L^{2}(\otimes^{2}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})}<+\infty
implies that
\mathcal{K}
considered as an operator from
L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
to itself is an Hilbert-Schmidt operator thus compact. This implies that
\mathcal{T}
is a Fredholm operator. We now show that the kernel of
\mathcal{T}:L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
is trivial. Let
v\in L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
such that
\mathcal{T}[v]=0

i.e.

v=\mathcal{K}[v]
, then
\mathcal{K}[v]
is in
H^{1}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
. Indeed we have

\mathcal{K}[v]^{\prime}(x)=-\mathcal{H}[v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}](x)-\dfrac{\mathrm{sgn}(x)}{1+|x|}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\in L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}).

Moreover since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{K}[v](x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)=0
, we conclude that
v\in\mathsf{H}
. We can now conclude that
v=0
by bijectivity of
\mathcal{T}
on
\mathsf{H}
. Finally, by Fredhom alternative,
\mathcal{T}
is invertible on
L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
since it is injective.

We now prove that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{T}\left[W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]=W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. We proceed by induction. For
n=0
, let
f\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\subset L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
. There exists a unique
v\in L^{2}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
such that
\mathcal{T}[v]=f
so
v=f+\mathcal{K}[v]
but since
f
and
