\mathcal{K}[v]
are bounded, so is
v\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. Reciprocally, if
v\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
so is
\mathcal{T}[v]
, hence
\mathcal{T}\left[L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. Finally, for all
v\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

\Big\|\mathcal{T}[v]\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\left(1+2P\max_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|k(x,y)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\right)\cdot\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

(83)

Now suppose
\mathcal{T}\left[W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]=W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is true and let’s show it for
n+1
. Let
f\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\subset W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, so by hypothesis, there exists
v\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that:

f^{(n)}(x)=v^{(n)}(x)+2P\left(\log(1+|.|)\right)^{(n)}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log|x-y|(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\\
+2P\delta_{n,0}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\dfrac{\log|z-y|}{1+|z|}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(z).

(84)

We deduce that
v^{(n)}
is differentiable of derivative

v^{(n+1)}(x)=f^{(n+1)}(x)-2P\left(\log(1+|.|)\right)^{(n+1)}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\mathcal{H}\left[(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}\right](x),

(85)

where
\left(\log(1+|.|)\right)^{(n)}(x)=\dfrac{n!\mathrm{sgn}(x)^{(n)}}{(1+|x|)^{n}}
. Since the two first terms in the RHS of (
85
) are clearly bounded, we just have to show that
\mathcal{H}\big[(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}\big]\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. By boundedness of
f^{(n+1)}
and
v^{(i)}
for all
i\leq n
, we have

(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n+1)}=\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\left(v^{(n+1)}+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n+1}{k}\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}^{(n+1-k)}}{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}v^{(k)}\right)\\
=\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\Bigg(f^{(n+1)}-2P\left(\log(1+|.|)\right)^{(n+1)}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\mathcal{H}\left[(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}\right]\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n+1}{k}\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}^{(n+1-k)}}{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}v^{(k)}\Bigg)\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}).

Thus, it holds that
\mathcal{H}\left[(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}\right]\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and that it is bounded. Hence it proves that
v^{(n+1)}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and hence that
W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\subset\mathcal{T}\left[W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]
. Conversely, if
v\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, then
f\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
by hypothesis and just as before, we show that (
85
) holds. We conclude that
f^{(n+1)}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
again by showing that
\mathcal{H}\left[(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(n)}\right]
is bounded by the fact that
v\in W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
. This establishes that
W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})=\mathcal{T}\left[W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\right]
.

Thus for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{T}:W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is a bijective operator. Furthermore, it is a bounded operator by the fact that for all
1\leq i\leq n
, there exists
C>0
such that for all
v\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, by Leibniz formula:

\big|\mathcal{T}[v]^{(i)}(x)\big|\leq\|v^{(i)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+2P\big\|\left(\log(1+|.|)\right)^{(i)}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\|v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\\
+2P\|v\|_{W_{i}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{i}\binom{i}{k}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big|\log\dfrac{|z-y|}{1+|z|}\Big|\cdot\Bigg|\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}^{(k)}}{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}(y)\Bigg|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\leq C\|v\|_{W_{i}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Above we used (
84
) and
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y=0
to deduce the following fact:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log|x-y|(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log|x-y|(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y-\log(1+|x|)\int_{\mathbb{R}}(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\dfrac{|x-y|}{1+|x|}(v\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})^{(i)}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y.

Thus, we conclude that
\max_{i\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket}\big\|\mathcal{T}[v]^{(i)}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C\|v\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
. The bound on
\big\|\mathcal{T}[v]\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
was shown in (
83
). We finally conclude that
\mathcal{T}:W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is bounded bijective between Banach spaces and by Banach isomorphism theorem so is
\mathcal{T}^{-1}
, this establishes (
82
) and completes the proof.

\square

Remark 6.3
.

An explicit expression for
\mathcal{T}^{-1}
is available using Fredholm determinant theory for invertible Hilbert-Schmidt operators, see
[
33
, Section XII]
. For all
v\in L^{2}_{0}(\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}})
,

\mathcal{T}^{-1}[v](x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}v(x)\\
+\dfrac{1}{\underset{2}{\det}(I-\mathcal{K})}\sum_{n\geq 1}\dfrac{(-1)^{n}}{n!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}\begin{vmatrix}k(x,s)&k(x,t_{1})&\dots&k(x,t_{n})\\
k(t_{1},s)&0&\dots&k(t_{1},t_{n})\\
\vdots&&&\vdots\\
k(t_{n},s)&k(t_{n},t_{1})&\ldots&0\end{vmatrix}v(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(s)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(t_{i}).

(86)

Above
\underset{2}{\det}
stands for the
2
-determinant. This formula was established in
[
27
, Theorem 6.11]

We are now able to show that
u_{t}
is a fixed point of a certain operator. We recall that
\phi
was introduced in the beginning of subsection
6.1
.

Proposition 6.4
.

For all
t\in[0,1]
,
u_{t}
defined in (
80
) is the unique measurable function such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}u_{t}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)=0
and which satisfies:

u_{t}=\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[u_{t}]

where
\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\phi+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\delta t\,\mathcal{U}_{t}[u]
, and

\mathcal{U}_{t}[v](x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\left(-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\mathcal{K}[v](x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{\log\Big(1+\delta tv(y)\Big)-\delta tv(y)}{\delta t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\right)^{2}\\
\times\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\exp\Bigg\{s\delta t\left(-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\mathcal{K}[v](x)\right)\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\log\Big(1+\delta tv(y)\Big)-\delta tv(y)}{\delta t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\Bigg\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{\log\Big(1+\delta tv(y)\Big)-\delta tv(y)}{(\delta t)^{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y).

Proof
.

Lemma
6.1
allows one to substitute
\lambda_{V_{\phi,t}}
in the representation for
\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}
by (
81
) hence leading to

\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}=(1+\delta tu_{t})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}=\exp\Bigg(-V_{\phi,t_{0}}-2PU^{\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}+\lambda_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}-\delta t\phi+\delta t\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\delta tU^{u_{t}\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}\\
-2P\delta t\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log|y-z|u_{t}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\log\Big(1+\delta tu_{t}(y)\Big)-\delta tu_{t}(y)\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\Bigg).

Identifying
\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
via the first three terms in the exponential,
u_{t}
has to satisfy the following relation for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

1+\delta tu_{t}(x)=\exp\Bigg\{\delta t\Big(-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\dfrac{|x-y|}{1+|x|}u_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\\
-2P\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log\dfrac{|y-z|}{1+|y|}u_{t}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\dfrac{1}{\delta t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\log\left(1+\delta tu_{t}(y)\right)-\delta tu_{t}(y)\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\Big)\Bigg\}.

Above, we have used that
-U^{u_{t}\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}(x)=\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\dfrac{|x-y|}{1+|x|}u_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)
which is justified by the fact that
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}u_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)=0
.
Conversely, any
u
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)=0
and satisfying the previous relation, verifies for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

V_{t}(x)+2PU^{w}(x)+\log w(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big\{V_{t}(y)+2PU^{w}(y)+\log w(y)\Big\}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)

where we have set
w\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(1+\delta tu)\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
. Because of this equation,
w
can be written in exponential form as in (
6
), it is thus positive and of mass
1
which makes
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}w(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x
a probability measure which satisfies the equation characterizing
\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}
. Thus, by uniqueness of the solution of (
5
),
\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}=\mu
and thus
u=u_{t}
.

We now expand
\exp
into its Taylor-integral series of order 2,
i.e.

e^{x}=1+x+x^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)e^{sx}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s
. By using that:

\mathcal{K}[v]=2P\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\dfrac{|x-y|}{1+|x|}u(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)-2P\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log\dfrac{|y-z|}{1+|y|}v(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(z)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)

and
\mathcal{T}[v]=v-\mathcal{K}[v]
, we get:

\mathcal{T}[u_{t}](x)=-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\\
+\delta t\Bigg[\left(-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}+\mathcal{K}[u_{t}](x)+\dfrac{1}{\delta t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[\log\left(1+\delta tu_{t}\right)-\delta tu_{t}\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\right)^{2}\\
\times\int_{0}^{1}\exp\left(s\delta t\left(-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}+\mathcal{K}[u_{t}](x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\log\left(1+\delta tu_{t}\right)-\delta tu_{t}}{\delta t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\right)\right)(1-s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\\
+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{\log\left(1+\delta tu_{t}\right)-\delta tu_{t}}{\left(\delta t\right)^{2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\Bigg].

We next use the invertibility of
\mathcal{T}
to conclude.

\square

The next theorem shows that for each
t
sufficiently close to
t_{0}
,
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}
is contractive on a ball of fixed radius. Let
n\geq 0
,
denote for all
R>0
,
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R)}
the closed ball of radius
R_{n}
for the
W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
-norm.

Theorem 6.5
.

For all
\varepsilon_{n}>0
small enough, there exists
R_{n}>0,
such that for all
t\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[
, the operator

\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}:\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})},\|.\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\right)\longrightarrow\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})},\|.\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\right)

is well-defined and continuous. Furthermore it is contractive, i.e. there exists
k_{n}\in]0,1[
, such that for all
v,w\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})}
,

\left\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[w]\right\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\leq k_{n}\|v-w\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}.

Moreover
k_{n}
is independent of
t
on
]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[
for
\varepsilon_{n}>0
small enough.

Proof
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
,
t\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon,t_{0}+\varepsilon[
,
v\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{0}(0,R)}
for an arbitrary
0<R<\dfrac{1}{\delta t}
(because otherwise the term
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log(1+\delta tv)\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}
in
\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]
might be ill-defined). We first show that
\|\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}<+\infty
.
First, by Taylor-Lagrange inequality, we deduce that

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\dfrac{\log\left[1+\delta tv(y)\right]-\delta tv(y)}{\delta t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\Big|\leq\dfrac{\|v\|_{{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}^{2}\delta t}{2}\sup_{|x|\leq\|v\|_{\infty}}\dfrac{1}{(1+\delta tx)^{2}}\leq\dfrac{R^{2}\delta t}{2(1-\delta tR)^{2}}.

By recalling the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{t}
in Proposition
6.4
and using the convexity of
x\mapsto x^{2}
, we get:

\left\|\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\delta t\Bigg[3\Bigg(4\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+R^{2}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\log\dfrac{|.-y|}{1+|.|}\right|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\delta t^{2}\dfrac{R^{4}}{4(1-\delta tR)^{4}}\Bigg)\\
\times\exp\left\{\delta t\left(2\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+R\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\log\dfrac{|.-y|}{1+|.|}\right|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\delta t\dfrac{R^{2}}{2(1-\delta tR)^{2}}\right)\right\}+\dfrac{R^{2}}{2(1-\delta tR)^{2}}\Bigg]\\
+2\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

The RHS is of the form
2\|\phi\|_{\infty}+\delta tg_{0}(\delta t)
where
g_{0}
is a positive function and with these notations:

\big\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{T},0}\left(2\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\delta tg_{0}(\delta t)\right).

Therefore, by choosing
t
such that
|t-t_{0}|<\varepsilon_{0}
for
\varepsilon_{0}>0
small enough, there exists
R_{0}>0
such that
2\|\phi\|_{\infty}C_{\mathcal{T},0}<R_{0}<\dfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}
and

\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{0}(0,R_{0})}\right)\subset\overline{\mathscr{B}_{0}(0,R_{0})}.

This makes the operator
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}:\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})},\|.\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\right)\longrightarrow\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})},\|.\|_{W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\right)
well-defined for all
|t-t_{0}|<\varepsilon_{0}
. For the contractivity, let
u,v\in\overline{\mathscr{B}(0,R_{0})}
, we get by Proposition
6.2
,
where
\mathcal{U}_{t}
was defined in Proposition
6.4
. We now want to control
\big|\mathcal{U}_{t}[u](x)-\mathcal{U}_{t}[v](x)\big|
. We have, by decomposing the sum:

\big|\mathcal{U}_{t}[u](x)-\mathcal{U}_{t}[v](x)\big|\leq\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}(x)\mathsf{E}[u](x)+\Delta_{3}(x)\mathfrak{h}[v](x)^{2}\right)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}[w](x)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\phi(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)+\mathcal{K}[w](x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\log\left(1+\delta tw(y)\right)-\delta tw(y)}{\delta t}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}[w](x)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\int_{0}^{1}\exp\Big[s\delta t\mathfrak{h}[w](x)\Big](1-s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\dfrac{\log(1+\delta tu(y))-\delta tu(y)-\log(1+\delta tv(y))+\delta tv(y)}{(\delta t)^{2}}\right|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\big|\mathfrak{h}[u](x)^{2}-\mathfrak{h}[v](x)^{2}\big|

\displaystyle\Delta_{3}(x)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\big|\mathsf{E}[u](x)-\mathsf{E}[v](x)\big|.

First, Taylor Lagrange inequality leads to:

\Delta_{1}\leq\sup_{y\in[-R_{0},R_{0}]}\dfrac{|y|}{1+\delta ty}\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\dfrac{R_{0}}{1-\delta tR_{0}}\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Furthermore, by using
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a+b)(a-b)
, that
\mathfrak{h}[u]
and
\mathfrak{h}[v]
are bounded

\Delta_{2}\leq 2\max\Big(\|\mathfrak{h}[u]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})},\|\mathfrak{h}[v]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\Big)\Bigg[\Big|\mathcal{K}[u-v](x)\Big|+\delta t\Delta_{1}\Bigg]\\
\leq\left(4\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+8PR_{0}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\log\dfrac{|.-y|}{1+|.|}\right|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\dfrac{\delta tR_{0}^{2}}{(1-\delta tR_{0})^{2}}\right)\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\\
\times\left(\dfrac{R_{0}\delta t}{1-\delta tR_{0}}+4P\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\log\dfrac{|.-y|}{1+|.|}\right|.\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\right)\leq C(R_{0})\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

Similarly, there exists
C(R_{0})>0
such that:

\Delta_{3}\|\mathfrak{h}[v]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(R_{0})\delta t\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

which finally leads to the existence of
C_{0}>0
such that:

\big\|\mathcal{U}_{t}[u]-\mathcal{U}_{t}[v]\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{0}\cdot|\delta t|\cdot\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

We now choose
\varepsilon_{0}
again small enough such that
k_{0}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\delta tC_{0}C_{\mathcal{T},0}<1
, this concludes the proof that
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}
is contractive on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{0}(0,R_{0})}
with contractivity constant
k_{0}
.

To get the contractivity property for
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}
on
W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, we adapt a similar strategy.
Let
u\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R)}
with
\delta tR<1
, by Proposition
6.2
,

\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}[\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]]\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{\mathcal{T},n}\|\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Furthermore, it is clear that every term appearing in the definition of
\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]
belongs to
W_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, thus by the same argument as before there exists a positive function
g_{n}
such that,

\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]\|_{W_{n}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{\mathcal{T},n}\Big(2\|\phi\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\delta tg_{n}(R)\Big).

We conclude just as before that by taking
\delta t
small enough,
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}:\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R)}\rightarrow\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R)}
is well-defined.
Finally, just as before since for all
u,v\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{t}[u]^{(n)}(x)

\displaystyle=\delta t\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}\left(\mathfrak{h}[u]^{2}\right)^{(k)}(x)\mathsf{E}[u]^{(n-k)}(x)

\displaystyle=\delta t\sum_{0\leq i\leq k\leq n}\binom{n}{k}\binom{k}{i}\mathfrak{h}[u]^{(i)}(x)\mathfrak{h}[u]^{(k-i)}(x)\mathsf{E}[u]^{(n-k)}(x).

Moreover, by the same controls as before it is easy to derive that for all
0\leq i\leq k\leq n
, for all
u,v\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})}
,

\|\mathfrak{h}[u]^{(i)}\mathfrak{h}[u]^{(k-i)}\mathsf{E}[u]^{(n-k)}-\mathfrak{h}[v]^{(i)}\mathfrak{h}[v]^{(k-i)}\mathsf{E}[v]^{(n-k)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(R_{n},n,i,k)\|u-v\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

This is enough to conclude that

\left\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[u]-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq\delta tC_{\mathcal{T},n}C(R_{n},n)\|u-v\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Finally, by taking
\delta t
small enough, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}
is contractive on
\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})}
with contractivity constant
k_{n}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\delta tC_{\mathcal{T},n}C(R_{n},n)<1
.

\square

Remark 6.6
.

Note that the definition of
u_{t}
as an element of
W^{\infty}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
depends on
t_{0}
and that we only proved the characterization of
u_{t}
as a fixed point for
t\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[\setminus\{t_{0}\}
with
\varepsilon_{n}>0
small enough (we stress that we successively lowered
\delta t
when increasing
n
.) Furthermore, since for all
v\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})

\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}[v]=-\phi+\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y),

then we can set
u_{t_{0}}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\phi+\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)
which is obviously the unique fixed point in
\displaystyle\bigcap_{n\in\mathbb{N}}W_{n}^{\infty}{(\mathbb{R})}
of
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{0}}
.

6.2
Regularity of the equilibrium measure

We now prove the continuity of
t\mapsto u_{t}\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon_{n}>0
and
R_{n}>0
be as in Theorem
6.5
. For all
v\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})}
, for all
(t_{p})_{p}\in\left(]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[\right)^{\mathbb{N}}
such that
t_{p}\underset{p\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}t\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[
then

\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{p}}[v]-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{p\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Proof
.

The proof is based on the fact that there exists a neighboorhood
U_{n}
of
t_{0}
such that for all
v\in\overline{\mathscr{B}_{n}(0,R_{n})}
,
t\in U_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{V}_{t}[v]\in W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is continuous. Since
\mathcal{T}^{-1}
is also continuous in
W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
-norm, we can conclude.

\square

Corollary 6.8
(Continuity of the fixed-point).

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
t,t^{\prime}\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[
,

\|u_{t}-u_{t^{\prime}}\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}0.

Proof
.

Let
(t_{n})_{n}\in\left(]t_{0}-\varepsilon,t_{0}+\varepsilon[\right)^{\mathbb{N}}
such that
t_{n}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}t\in]t_{0}-\varepsilon_{n},t_{0}+\varepsilon_{n}[
. First

\left\|u_{t_{n}}-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\|\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t_{n}}]-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq k_{i}\left\|u_{t_{n}}-u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

and by the triangle inequality, we obtain:

\left\|u_{t}-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\geq\left\|u_{t_{n}}-u_{t}\right\|_{\infty}-\left\|u_{t_{n}}-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\geq(1-k_{n})\left\|u_{t_{n}}-u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Nevertheless by Lemma
6.7
,
\left\|u_{t}-\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t_{n}}[u_{t}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
because
u_{t}
is the fixed point of
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\circ\mathcal{V}_{t}
which is a continuous operator with respect to
t
hence
\left\|u_{t_{n}}-u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
.

\square

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
t,t_{0}\in[0,1]
,

\boxed{\left\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.}

Furthermore, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\mapsto\partial_{x}^{k}\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
and satisfies the following partial integro-differential equation:

\partial_{t}\partial_{x}^{k}\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)=\partial_{x}^{k}\left[\left(-\phi+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(s)\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\right)\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}\right](x).

Proof
.

By setting
u_{t}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t^{\prime}}}}{t-t^{\prime}}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V_{\phi,t^{\prime}}}}
,

\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t^{\prime}}}\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=|t-t^{\prime}|\cdot\left\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t^{\prime}}}u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq 2^{n}|t-t^{\prime}|\cdot\left\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t^{\prime}}}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

By Corollary
6.8
,
\left\|u_{t}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}\left\|u_{t^{\prime}}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
, thus the right-hand side goes to zero proving the claim.

For the second point, we notice that

\left\|\dfrac{\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}}{\delta t}-\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}u_{t_{0}}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=\left\|(u_{t}-u_{t_{0}})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq 2^{n}\|u_{t}-u_{t_{0}}\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\cdot\|\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Since the RHS goes to zero as
t\rightarrow t_{0}
,
u_{t_{0}}=-\phi+\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(y)
and
n
is arbitrary, we conclude that,
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t\mapsto\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)
is differentiable at every
t\in[0,1]
of derivative

\partial_{t}\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}^{(k)}(x)=-\left(\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}\phi\right)^{(k)}(x)+\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}^{(k)}(x)\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}(y).

Since the above expression is again differentiable in
t
(one deals with the integral by dominated convergence theorem with the domination
|\phi(x)\partial_{t}\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)|\leq 2\|\phi\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)
for an arbitrary
t_{0}
), we conclude that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t\mapsto\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

\square

Corollary 6.10
(Convergence of moments).

Let
h\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in[0,1]
by denoting
m_{t}(h)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}x^{h}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)
, we have for all
t_{0}\in[0,1]

m_{t}(h)\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\rightarrow}m_{t_{0}}(h).

Proof
.

For all
t\in[0,1]
, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
\rho_{V_{\phi,t}}(x)\leq(1+\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty})\rho_{V_{\phi,t_{0}}}(x)
, hence by dominated convergence theorem
|m_{t}(h)-m_{t_{0}}(h)|\underset{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}0
.

\square

7
Proof of Theorem
1.4

In this section, we prove, using Mehta’s explicit formula, the expansion of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]
. We then deduce Theorem
1.4
from the latter and from Theorems
1.2
and
1.3
.

7.1
Expansion of the partition function for the Gaussian potential

The asymptotic behaviour of
\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]
can be deduced from Mehta’s formula
[
48
, 17.6.7]

\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]=(2\pi)^{N/2}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\dfrac{\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{iP}{N}\right)}{\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{P}{N}\right)}.

(87)

This will allow us to use this formula in our interpolating integration formula to deduce the asymptotic expansion of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V_{\phi}]
. From the previous equation, we can deduce the asymptotic behaviour of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]
. It is given by the following theorem:

Theorem 7.1
.

There exists a sequence
(g_{k})_{k\geq 0}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
, such that for all
K\geq 0
,

\boxed{\dfrac{1}{N}\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]=\sum_{i=0}^{K}\dfrac{g_{i}(P)}{N^{i}}+O\left(N^{-(K+1)}\right)}

(88)

with

g_{1}(P)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\gamma\dfrac{P}{2}+\dfrac{\log(1+P)}{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\log\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)-\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)-\dfrac{P}{j}\right).

(89)

Above
\gamma
denotes the Euler-Mascheroni constant.

Proof
.

We first use (
87
) to deduce

\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]=\dfrac{N\log(2\pi)}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{iP}{N}\right)-N\log\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{P}{N}\right).

(90)

Let
K>0
, using the Taylor series expansion of
\log\Gamma
around 1 (see
[
34
, 8.342]
), one has

-N\log\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{P}{N}\right)=\gamma P-\sum_{k=1}^{+\infty}\dfrac{\zeta(k+1)}{k+1}\dfrac{(-P)^{k+1}}{N^{k}}.

(91)

where
\zeta
denotes the Riemann
\zeta
function. The second term in (
90
) can be estimated by using the Weierstrass product formula for
\dfrac{1}{\Gamma}
:

\dfrac{1}{\Gamma(z)}=e^{\gamma z}z\prod_{j=1}^{+\infty}(1+\dfrac{z}{j})e^{-z/j}

(92)

which is valid for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
. Hence we deduce that:

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\log\Gamma\left(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\right)=-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\gamma\Big(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\Big)+\log\left(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\right)-S_{N}(k)\right)\\
=-\gamma N-\gamma\dfrac{(N+1)P}{2}-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\log\left(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\right)+\sum_{k=1}^{N}S_{N}(k)

(93)

where
S_{N}(k)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\left[\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}+\dfrac{kP}{Nj}\right)-\dfrac{1}{j}-\dfrac{kP}{Nj}\right]
.
By the Euler-Maclaurin formula, we have the following identity for any
K>0
:

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\log\left(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\right)=\int_{0}^{N}f_{N}(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=1}^{K+2}\dfrac{B_{k}}{k!}\left(f_{N}^{(k-1)}(N)-f_{N}^{(k-1)}(0)\right)+R_{K+2}^{(N)}

(94)

where
f_{N}(x)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\log\left(1+\dfrac{xP}{N}\right)
and
B_{k}
is the
k
-th Bernoulli number. The remainder
R_{K+2}^{(N)}
is defined by
R_{K+2}^{(N)}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(-1)^{K+1}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{N}f_{N}^{(K+2)}(t)\dfrac{\widetilde{B}_{K+2}(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)}{(K+2)!}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
, where
\widetilde{B}_{K+2}
is the
(K+2)
-th Bernoulli polynomial. By using the following bound on Bernoulli polynomials,

\forall x\in[0,1],\forall k>0,\hskip 42.67912pt|\widetilde{B}_{k}(x)|\leq 2\dfrac{k!}{(2\pi)^{k}}\zeta(k)

where
\zeta
is the Riemann zeta function,
R_{K+2}^{(N)}
can be controlled by the following inequalities:

\displaystyle|R_{K+2}^{(N)}|\leq\dfrac{2\zeta(K+2)}{(2\pi)^{K+2}}\int_{0}^{N}|f_{N}^{(K+2)}(t)|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\zeta(K+2)}{(2\pi)^{K+2}}\int_{0}^{N}\dfrac{P^{K+2}}{N^{K+2}}\dfrac{(K+1)!}{\left(1+\dfrac{P}{N}t\right)^{K+2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\dfrac{2\zeta(K+2)(K+1)!}{(2\pi)^{K+2}}\dfrac{P^{K+1}}{N^{K+1}}\int_{0}^{P}\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}u}{\left(1+u\right)^{K+2}}=O\Big(N^{-{(K+1)}}\Big).

Extracting the large-
N
behaviour in (
94
) leads to

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\log\left(1+\dfrac{kP}{N}\right)=\dfrac{N}{P}\int_{0}^{P}\log(1+t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t+B_{1}\log(1+P)\\
+\sum_{k=2}^{K+2}\dfrac{B_{k}}{k!}(-1)^{k}(k-2)!\dfrac{P^{k-1}}{N^{k-1}}\left(\dfrac{1}{(1+P)^{k-1}}-1\right)+O\Big(N^{-(K+1)}\Big)\\
=Nc_{-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}\dfrac{c_{k}}{N^{k}}+O\Big(N^{-(K+1)}\Big)

(95)

where
c_{-1}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}(1+P^{-1})\log(1+P)-1
,
c_{0}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\dfrac{\log(1+P)}{2}
and for all
k\in\llbracket 1,K\rrbracket
,

c_{k}=\dfrac{-B_{k+1}(-P)^{k}}{k(k+1)}\left(\dfrac{1}{(1+P)^{k}}-1\right).

Also by Fubini’s theorem, we get,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}S_{N}(k)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\left\{\sum_{k=1}^{N}\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}+\dfrac{kP}{Nj}\right)-\dfrac{1}{j}-\dfrac{kP}{Nj}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\left\{\dfrac{N}{j}+\dfrac{(N+1)P}{2j}+\sum_{k=1}^{N}g_{N,j}(k)\right\}

where
g_{N,j}(x)=\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}+\dfrac{P}{jN}x\right)
. The first equality clearly shows that the RHS is a serie of general term bounded by
O\left(j^{-2}\right)
, so it converges and justifies the application of Fubini’s theorem. Again by Euler-Maclaurin formula, we get:

\sum_{k=1}^{N}g_{N,j}(k)=-\int_{0}^{N}\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}+t\dfrac{P}{Nj}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t-\dfrac{1}{2}\left[\log\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)-\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)\right]\\
-\sum_{k=2}^{K+2}\dfrac{(-1)^{k}B_{k}}{k(k-1)}\left\{\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)^{1-k}-\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)^{1-k}\right\}\left(\dfrac{P}{Nj}\right)^{k-1}+R_{K+2}^{(N)}(j)

(96)

where again the new remainder
R_{K+2}^{(N)}(j)
can be controlled
via

|R_{K+2}^{(N)}(j)|\leq\dfrac{2\zeta(K+2)}{(2\pi)^{K+2}}\int_{0}^{N}\dfrac{(K+1)!\left(\dfrac{P}{Nj}\right)^{K+2}}{\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}+t\dfrac{P}{jN}\right)^{K+2}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\\
=\dfrac{2\zeta(K+2)}{(2\pi)^{K+2}}\left(\dfrac{P}{Nj}\right)^{K+1}\left\{\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)^{-(K+1)}-\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)^{-(K+1)}\right\}=O\Bigg(\dfrac{1}{(Nj)^{K+1}}\Bigg)

where
O\left(\dfrac{1}{j^{K+1}}\right)
depends on
K
and
P
but not on
N
. Hence we deduce that

\sum_{k=1}^{N}g_{N,j}(k)=-N\int_{0}^{1}\log\left(1+\dfrac{1+sP}{j}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s-\dfrac{1}{2}\left[\log\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)-\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)\right]\\
+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\dfrac{(-P)^{k}B_{k+1}}{k(k+1)}\left\{\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)^{-k}-\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)^{-k}\right\}\dfrac{1}{(Nj)^{k}}+O\left((jN)^{-(K+1)}\right).

This leads to:

\sum_{k=1}^{N}S_{N}(k)=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\left[\dfrac{N}{j}+\dfrac{(N+1)P}{2j}+\sum_{k=1}^{N}g_{N,j}(k)\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{+\infty}\left(u_{j}^{(1)}N+\sum_{k=0}^{K}u_{j}^{(k)}N^{-k}\right)+O(N^{-(K+1)})\\
=d_{1}N+\sum_{k=0}^{K}d_{-k}N^{-k}+O\Big(N^{-(K+1)}\Big)

(97)

where for all
k=-1,0\dots K
,
\left(u_{j}^{(k)}\right)_{j>0}\in\ell^{1}(\mathbb{N}^{*})
and
d_{-k}\in\mathbb{R}
. This establishes the existence of the asymptotic expansion of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]
up to
O\Big(N^{-(K+1)}\Big)
. Collecting the different terms, leads to the formula for
g_{1}(P)
.

\square

7.2
Free energy of the model

Only, in this subsection, since the parameter
P
varies, we include the
P
-dependence of
\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V]
in the notation and write
\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{P}[V]
instead. The following result exhibits that there is a logarithmitc divergence of the free energy when
P\rightarrow 0
and hence a sort of phase transition.

Theorem 7.2
(Free energy formula for Gaussian Potential).

Let
P>0
, the free energy associated with the Gaussian potential is

F(P)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-1}\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{P}\left[2PV_{G}\right]=-\dfrac{1+P}{2}\log(2P)+\dfrac{\log(2\pi)}{2}+\int_{0}^{1}\log\Gamma\left(1+Px\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x

(98)

As
P
goes to
+\infty
, we have:

F(P)=-P\left(\dfrac{3+\log 2}{2}\right)-\dfrac{1+\log 2}{2}+\log(2\pi)+\dfrac{\log P}{12P}+O\left(P^{-1}\right)

(99)

Proof
.

By a change of variable, it holds that
\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{P}\left[2PV_{G}\right]=\left(\sqrt{2P}\right)^{-N-P(N-1)}\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{P}\left[V_{G}\right].
Hence by Mehta’s formula (
87
),

F(P)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-1}\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{P}\left[2PV_{G}\right]=-\dfrac{1+P}{2}\log(2P)+\dfrac{\log(2\pi)}{2}+\int_{0}^{1}\log\Gamma\left(1+Px\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x.

We can replace the last term by its asymptotic expansion so that

\int_{0}^{1}\log\Gamma\left(1+Px\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x=\dfrac{(P+1)}{2}\log P-\dfrac{3P}{2}+\dfrac{\log(2\pi)-1}{2}+\dfrac{1}{12P}\log P+O\left(P^{-1}\right).

We used the classic formula to conclude

\log\Gamma(1+Px)=(1+Px)\log(1+Px)-1-Px-\dfrac{\log(1+Px)-\log(2\pi)}{2}+\dfrac{1}{12(1+Px)}+O\left(\dfrac{1}{(1+Px)^{3}}\right).

\square

7.3
Interpolation with general potential

We first establish the link between the 1-linear statistics and the partition function with general potential and the one with Gaussian potential.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
V_{t}(x)=tV(x)+(1-t)V_{G}(x)
with
t\in[0,1]
. We have

\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]}=-N\int_{0}^{1}\Braket{V-V_{G}}_{\mu_{N}}^{V_{t}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t

(100)

Proof
.

By the fundamental theorem of calculus:

\log\dfrac{\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V]}{\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V_{t}]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t=-\int_{0}^{1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\mathbb{R}^{N}}p_{N}^{V_{t}}(\underline{x})\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{t}V_{t}(x_{i})\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}^{N}\underline{x}.

Since
\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{t}V_{t}(x_{i})=N\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[V(x)-V_{G}(x)\right]d\mu_{N}(x)
, where
\mu_{N}
is the empricial measure associated to the external potential
V_{t}
, it concludes the proof.

\square

Theorem 7.4
.

For all
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, there exists a sequence
(c_{i})_{i\geq 0}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}
depending on
\phi
and
P
such that for all
K\geq 0

\dfrac{1}{N}\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G,\phi}\right]=\sum_{i=0}^{K}\dfrac{c_{i}}{N^{i}}+O\left(N^{-(K+1)}\right).

The leading term
c_{0}
is equal to the following expression:

\int_{\mathbb{R}}V_{G,\phi}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{G,\phi}}(x)-P\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log|x-y|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{G,\phi}}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{G,\phi}}(y)+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\log\left(\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{G,\phi}}(x)}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V_{G,\phi}}(x).

The subleading term
c_{1}
can be written as

c_{1}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\gamma\dfrac{P}{2}+\dfrac{\log(1+P)}{2}+\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j\geq 1}\left(\log\left(1+\dfrac{P+1}{j}\right)-\log\left(1+\dfrac{1}{j}\right)-\dfrac{P}{j}\right)\\
-P\int_{0}^{1}\left[\Braket{\partial_{1}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\phi}_{\mu_{V_{G,\phi,t}}}+\Braket{\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}\left[\partial_{2}\mathcal{D}\circ\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}\phi\right]}_{\mu_{V_{G,\phi,t}}}\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t.

(101)

Proof
.

By Lemma
7.3
and Theorem
7.1
, to establish the asymptotic expansion of
\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G,\phi}\right]
, it suffices to obtain the one for
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\Braket{\phi}_{\mu_{N}}^{V_{G,\phi,t}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
. By Theorem
5.2
, we get, with
(d_{i}^{(1),t})
the coefficients appearing in the expansion under the choice of potential
V_{G,\phi,t}
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\Braket{\phi}_{\mu_{N}}^{V_{G,\phi,t}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\Braket{\phi}_{\mu_{V_{G,\phi,t}}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{i=1}^{K}\dfrac{\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}d_{i}^{(1),t}(\phi)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{N^{i}}+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left(\Braket{\phi}_{\Delta\mu_{N}}^{V_{G,\phi,t}}-\sum_{i=1}^{K}\dfrac{d_{i}^{(1),t}(\phi)}{N^{i}}\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t.

Finally, we conclude that the last integral is a
O\left(N^{-(K+1)}\right)
by (
76
) and the continuity of the map
t\mapsto C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V_{G,\phi,t})
obtained in Proposition
B.11
. Furthermore, by collecting order
1
for
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}\left[V_{G}\right]
and
\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}d_{i}^{(1),V_{G,\phi,t}}(\phi)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
for
i=1
in Theorem
5.2
, we infer on the value of
c_{1}
.

\square

8
Conclusion and open questions

This work adapted the loop equations method to prove the existence of a
N^{-1}
asymptotic expansion for the partition function, see Theorem
1.4
, for a general class of potential. This class includes all potentials of the form
x^{2}+\phi
where
\phi
is a smooth bounded function. An immediate continuation of this result would be to extend it to more general confining potentials like
x^{4}
for example. Another natural question would be to extend these ideas to more general interactions (Riesz, Coulomb, 2D-log in the spirit of
[
57
, Theorem 2]
). Finally, our result shows that, contrary to
[
16
]
, there is no oscillatory terms in the expansion of
\log\mathcal{Z}_{N}[V_{G,\phi,t}]
since there is no multi-cut situation in the high temperature regime.

Appendix A
Appendix: Lemmas and technical results

The first lemma in this appendix recalls several useful properties of the Hilbert transfrom used throughout this article.

Lemma A.1
(Properties of the Hilbert transform).

i)

As a consequence,
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{H}
is an isometry of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, and
\mathcal{H}
satisfies on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
the identity
\mathcal{H}^{2}=-\pi^{2}I
.

ii)

Derivative: For any
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
,
\mathcal{H}[f]
is also
H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathcal{H}[f]^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}[f^{\prime}]
.

iii)

For all
p>1
, the Hilbert transform can be extended as a bounded operator
\mathcal{H}:L^{p}(\mathbb{R})\to L^{p}(\mathbb{R})
.

iv)

Skew-self adjointness: For any
f,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
,
\Braket{\mathcal{H}[f],g}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=-\langle f,\mathcal{H}[g]\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
.

v)

For all
\delta>0
, for all
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
such that
f^{\prime}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,
\|\mathcal{H}[f]\|_{\infty}\leq(\delta^{-1}\|f\|_{1}+2\delta\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty})

Proof
.

We refer to
[
40
]
for the proofs of properties
i)-iv)
. To prove
v)
, let
f
be such a function,

|\mathcal{H}[f](x)|\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big|\int_{\varepsilon\leq|x-y|\leq\delta}\dfrac{f(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y-x}\Big|+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big|\int_{\delta\leq|x-y|\leq\varepsilon^{-1}}\dfrac{f(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y-x}\Big|.

The second term in the RHS can be bounded by
\delta^{-1}\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}
while the first term verifies

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Big|\int_{\varepsilon\leq|x-y|\leq\delta}\dfrac{f(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y-x}|\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\varepsilon\leq|x-y|\leq\delta}\Bigg|\dfrac{f(y)-f(x)}{y-x}\Bigg|\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y+\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\Bigg|f(x)\int_{\varepsilon\leq|x-y|\leq\delta}\dfrac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y-x}\Bigg|.

The first term in the RHS can be bounded by
2\delta\|f^{\prime}\|_{\infty}
while the second is equal to
0
. This allows to conclude.

\square

The following result is proven in
[
27
, Lemma 2.3]
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
be such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
exists and let
f:t\mapsto tu(t)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
then

\mathcal{H}[u](x)\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\dfrac{-\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{x}.

Moreover if
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t=0
,
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
exists and
g:t\mapsto t^{2}u(t)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R})
, then

\mathcal{H}[u](x)\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}\displaystyle\dfrac{-\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}tu(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t}{x^{2}}.

As a consequence, we obtain that
\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}](x)\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{\sim}-x^{-1}
and the logarithmic potential
U^{\rho_{V}}
is Lipschitz bounded, with bounded derivative
\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]
.

We now state and prove an inequality on the norm of the inverse of the master operator multiplied by the equlibrium density in Sobolev spaces. This inequality is crucial in the controls of Section
4
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
n\geq 1
, and
h\in H^{n}(\mathbb{R})
,

\|\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{3}(V,n)\|h\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R})}.

with a constant
C_{3}(V,n),
only depending on
V
and
n
. For the choice of potentiel
V=V_{\phi,t}
, for
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
with
\phi^{(k)}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in[0,1]
,
t\mapsto C_{3}(V_{\phi,t},n)
is a continuous function.

Moreover, for all
h\in H^{n}(\mathbb{R})\cap W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
,

\|\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{5}(V,n)\|h\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

with a constant
C_{5}(V,n),
only depending on
V
and
n
. The function
t\mapsto C_{5}(V_{\phi,t},n)
is also continuous.

Proof
.

Recall that
\theta=(\log\rho_{V})^{\prime}
, we first prove that for all
k\geq 0
,
h\in\dfrac{1}{\rho_{V}}H^{k}(\mathbb{R})
, there exists finite sets of indices
\mathfrak{I}_{l,a}^{k}
,
\mathfrak{J}^{k}_{l}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{l}^{k}
independent of
V
and polynomials

p_{a,1,1}^{k}
,
p_{a,b,c,d}^{k}
,
q_{i,b,c}^{k}
in
\theta,\dots,\theta^{(k-1)}
, with coefficients independent of
V
and of degree at most
k
, such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]^{(k)}=\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}p_{i,1,1}^{k}h^{(i)}+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{I}^{k}_{2,i}}p_{i,j,2,1}^{k}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}p_{i,j,2,2}^{k}h^{(i)}\right]+\ldots\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{I}^{k}_{k,i}}p_{i,j,k,1}^{k}\mathcal{H}\Bigg[\rho_{V}p_{i,j,k,2}^{k}\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}p_{i,j,k,3}^{k}\mathcal{H}\Big[\ldots\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}p_{i,j,k,k}^{k}h^{(i)}\underbrace{\Big]\Big]\ldots\Bigg]}_{k-1}+q^{k}_{1,1}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\\
+\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{J}_{2}^{k}}q^{k}_{j,2,1}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}q^{k}_{j,2,2}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right]+\ldots+\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{J}_{k+1}^{k}}q^{k}_{j,k+1,1}\mathcal{H}\Bigg[\rho_{V}q^{k}_{j,k+1,2}\mathcal{H}\Big[\ldots\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}q^{k}_{j,k+1,k+1}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\underbrace{\Big]\ldots\Bigg]}_{k}\\
+\Bigg(r^{k}_{1,1}+\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{K}^{k}_{2}}r_{j,2,1}^{k}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}r_{j,2,2}^{k}\right]+\ldots+\sum_{j\in\mathfrak{K}^{k}_{k}}r^{k}_{j,k,1}\mathcal{H}\Bigg[\rho_{V}r^{k}_{j,k,2}\mathcal{H}\Big[\ldots\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}r^{k}_{j,k,k}\underbrace{\Big]\ldots\Bigg]}_{k-1}\Bigg)\\
\times\left(2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right]\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}\right).

(102)

We prove it by induction, where for
n=1
one just uses the definition of
\Xi
for the initial case
i.e.

\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{\prime}=h-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h(t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t)-\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}{\rho_{V}}\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{\prime}-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right]+2P\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right](t)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}(t).

For the induction step, we use a bootstrap argument. Suppose (
102
) holds at rank
k
, then differentiate and replace
\left(\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\right)^{\prime}
by the RHS of the above relation to show that (
102
) holds at rank
k+1
.

Now, by the Leibniz formula, for all
k\in\llbracket 0,n\rrbracket
, it holds that

\left(\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right)^{(k)}=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k}{i}\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}\Xi^{-1}[h]^{(i)}.

Furthermore by (
102
), by using successively that
\pi^{-1}\mathcal{H}
is an isometry of
L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, inequality (
30
) and Jensen’s inequality, we obtain:

\max_{0\leq k\leq n}\|\left(\rho_{V}\Xi^{-1}[h]\right)^{(k)}(x)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{3}(V,n)\cdot\|h\|_{H^{k}(\mathbb{R})}

with
C_{3}(V,n)
given by

C_{3}(V,n)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C(n)\\
\times\max_{i\leq k\leq n}\Bigg\{i\max_{0\leq a<i}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}p_{a,b,1,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}+i\pi\max_{0\leq a<i}|\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{2,a}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{2,a}}\left(\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}p_{a,b,2,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}p_{a,b,2,2}^{i}\|_{\infty}\right)+\dots{\\
}+i\pi^{i-1}\max_{0\leq a<i}|\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{i,a}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{i,a}}\left(\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}p_{a,b,i,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}.\prod_{l=2}^{i}\|\rho_{V}p_{a,b,i,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\right)+C_{\mathcal{L}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{1,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}\|_{\infty}\\
+C_{\mathcal{L}}\pi|\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{2}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{2}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{b,2,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}q_{b,2,2}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}\|_{\infty}+\dots{\\
}+C_{\mathcal{L}}\pi^{i}|\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{i+1}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{i+1}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{b,i+1,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=2}^{i}\|\rho_{V}q_{b,i+1,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}q_{b,i+1,i+1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}\|_{\infty}\\
+2\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}\left(1+2P\pi C_{\mathcal{L}}\right)\Big[\|r_{1,1}^{i}\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}}{\rho_{V}}\sqrt{\rho_{V}}\|_{\infty}+\pi|\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{2}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{2}}\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}r_{b,2,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}r_{b,2,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}+\dots{\\
}+\pi^{i-1}|\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{i}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{i}}\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}r_{b,i,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=2}^{i-1}\|\rho_{V}r_{b,i,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}r_{b,i,i}^{i}\|_{\infty}\Big]\Bigg\}

(103)

For the second inequality, if
h\in H^{n}(\mathbb{R})\cap W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
, we use the fact the same inequalities but we use the following integrals at the end
\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(p_{a,b,i,l}^{i}h^{(a)}\rho_{V}(t)\right)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t\leq\|h\|_{W_{a}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}(p_{a,b,i,l}^{i}\rho_{V_{t}})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}t
. This leads to

\|\rho_{V}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[h]\|_{H^{n}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{5}(V,n)\|h\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

with
C_{5}(V,n)
given by

C_{5}(V,n)\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}C(n)\\
\times\max_{i\leq k\leq n}\Bigg\{i\max_{0\leq a<i}\left\|\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}}{\rho_{V}}p_{a,b,1,1}^{i}\sqrt{\rho_{V}}\right\|_{\infty}+i\pi\max_{0\leq a<i}|\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{2,a}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{2,a}}\left(\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}p_{a,b,2,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}p_{a,b,2,2}^{i}\|_{\infty}\right)+\dots{\\
}+i\pi^{i-1}\max_{0\leq a<i}|\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{i,a}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{I}^{i}_{i,a}}\left(\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}p_{a,i,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}.\prod_{l=2}^{i-1}\|\rho_{V}p_{a,i,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}p_{a,i,i}^{i}\|_{\infty}\right)\\
+C_{\mathcal{L}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{1,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}+C_{\mathcal{L}}\pi|\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{2}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{2}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{b,2,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}q_{b,2,2}^{i}\|_{\infty}+\dots{\\
}+C_{\mathcal{L}}\pi^{i}|\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{i+1}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{J}^{i}_{i+1}}\left\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}q_{b,i+1,1}^{i}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=2}^{i}\|\rho_{V}q_{b,i+1,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{V}^{1/2}q_{b,i+1,i+1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\\
+2\left(1+2P\pi\|\rho_{V}\|_{\infty}^{1/2}C_{\mathcal{L}}\right)\Big[\|r_{1,1}^{i}\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}}{\rho_{V}}\sqrt{\rho_{V}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}+\pi|\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{2}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{2}}\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}r_{b,2,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}r_{b,2,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}+\dots{\\
}+\pi^{i-1}|\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{i}|\max_{b\in\mathfrak{K}^{i}_{i}}\|\rho_{V}^{(k-i)}r_{b,i,1}^{i}\|_{\infty}\prod_{l=2}^{i-1}\|\rho_{V}r_{b,i,l}^{i}\|_{\infty}\|\sqrt{\rho_{V}}r_{b,i,i}^{i}\|_{\infty}\Big]\Bigg\}.

(104)

The fact that
t\mapsto C_{i}(V_{\phi,t},n)
for
i=3
or
5
is shown in Appendix
B
.

\square

Remark A.4
.

With
\theta=\dfrac{\rho_{V}^{\prime}}{\rho_{V}}
,
g\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\widetilde{\Xi^{-1}}[f]
and
c\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]-f\right)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}\mu_{V}
we have

\displaystyle g^{\prime}

\displaystyle=f-\theta g-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]+c

\displaystyle g^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=-\theta f+f^{\prime}-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}f\right]+\left(\theta^{2}-\theta^{\prime}\right)g+2P\theta\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]+4P^{2}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\left(-\theta-2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]\right)c.

\displaystyle g^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\left(\theta^{2}-2\theta^{\prime}\right)f-\theta f^{\prime}+f^{\prime\prime}+2P\theta\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}f\right]-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\theta f\right]-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}f^{\prime}\right]+4P^{2}\mathcal{H}\big[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}f\right]\Big]

\displaystyle+\left[\left(\theta^{2}-\theta^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\theta^{3}-\theta\theta^{\prime}\right)\right]g+2P\left(\theta^{2}-2\theta^{\prime}\right)\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}g]-4P^{2}\theta\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]\right]+4P^{2}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\theta\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]\right]

\displaystyle+4P^{2}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}\theta g\right]\right]-8P^{3}\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\big[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{V}g\right]\big]\Big]

\displaystyle+\left((\theta^{2}-2\theta^{\prime})+2P\theta\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]-2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}\theta]+4P^{2}\mathcal{H}\Big[\rho_{V}\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]\right]\Big)

Appendix B
Integrability of the constants

B.1
Parameter continuity of norms of certain functions

In this appendix, we work with
V=V_{G,\phi,t}
,
t\in[0,1]
and
\phi\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.
We will show, that the constant
C_{i}(V_{G,\phi,t})
appearing in our problem, see Theorem
4.13
, Theorem
4.14
and Theorem
4.17
will be continuous in
t
hence integrable on
[0,1]
. By using the main result from Section
6
,
i.e.
Theorem
6.9
stating that the map
t\mapsto\|\rho_{t}\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
(setting
\rho_{t}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\rho_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
for this appendix) is continuous for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we will show the following lemma.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
t,t_{0}\in[0,1]
, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}-\rho_{t_{0}}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.

Proof
.

We prove it by induction and use Lemma
A.1
and Theorem
6.9
. For
n=0
, we know that there exists
C>0
, such that:

\left\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}-\rho_{t_{0}}]\right\|_{\infty}\leq C\left(\|\rho_{t}-\rho_{t_{0}}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+\|\rho_{t}^{\prime}-\rho_{t_{0}}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}).

By Scheffé’s lemma, the
L^{1}
norm goes to zero and by Theorem
6.9
goes also to zero as
t
goes to
t_{0}
. Now suppose that
\left\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}-\rho_{t_{0}}]\right\|_{W_{n}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0
for some
n\geq 0
. We have that:

\left\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}^{(n+1)}-\rho_{t_{0}}^{(n+1)}]\right\|_{\infty}\leq C\left(\left\|\rho_{t}^{(n+1)}-\rho_{t_{0}}^{(n+1)}\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}+\left\|\rho_{t}^{(n+2)}-\rho_{t_{0}}^{(n+2)}\right\|_{\infty}\right).

The second term on the RHS goes to zero by Theorem
6.9
. For the first term, since for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho_{t_{0}}^{(k)}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
and we have the following domination by Leibniz formula:

|\rho_{t}^{(n+1)}(x)|\leq(1+\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{W_{n+1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})})\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}|\rho_{t_{0}}^{(k)}(x)|,

we can conclude by the dominated convergence theorem that the
L^{1}
-norm goes to zero. The fact that
u_{s}
is uniformly bounded comes from Corollary
6.8
.

\square

Secondly, we set for all
t\in[0,1]
,
M_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
(see Lemma
4.8
) equal to any value
M
such that:

M>\max\left(1+\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+2P\max_{t\in[0,1]}\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}]\|_{\infty},2\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+2P\max_{t\in[0,1]}\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}]\|_{\infty}\right)\right).

It is well-defined because of Lemma
B.1
. The following Lemma will be useful to show some controls on different quantities below. We recall that
\theta_{t}=(\log\rho_{t})^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{t}=\dfrac{1}{\theta_{t}}
.

Lemma B.2
.

For all
t\in[0,1]
, for all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, for all
|x|\geq M
,

\bigg|\theta_{t}(x)\bigg|\geq 1\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ and }\hskip 14.22636pt\Big|\alpha_{t}^{(i)}(x)\Big|^{j}\leq\delta_{i,0}\dfrac{C_{0,j}}{|x|^{j}}+\dfrac{C_{i,j}}{|x|^{2j}}

for constants
C_{i,j}>0
independent of
t
.

Proof
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
-\theta_{t}(x)=x+t\phi^{\prime}(x)+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x).
Thus if
|x|\geq M
,

\bigg|\dfrac{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}{\rho_{t}}(x)\bigg|\geq 1+\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}-t|\phi^{\prime}(x)|\right)+2P\left(\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{s}]\|_{\infty}-|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x)|\right)\geq 1.

For the second point, one notices by differentiation and (
23
) that there exists polynomials
\mathsf{P}_{k}
with coefficients independent of
t
such that

\alpha_{t}^{(i)}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{i}\dfrac{\mathsf{P}_{k}\left(t\phi^{\prime}(x),\dots,t\phi^{(i+1)}(x),\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x),\dots,\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{t}^{(i)}\right](x)\right)}{\left(x+t\phi^{\prime}(x)+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x)\right)^{k+1}}.

(105)

Furthermore, since
|x|\geq 2\left(\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+2P\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,1]}\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}]\|_{\infty}\right)
, we have:

\Big|x+t\phi^{\prime}(x)+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x)\Big|\geq\dfrac{|x|}{2}+\left(\dfrac{|x|}{2}-\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}-2P\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{s}]\|_{\infty}\right)\geq\dfrac{|x|}{2}.

Finally, the whole dependence in
t
and
x
of the numerator in (
105
) are in the entries which are bounded uniformly in
t
and
x
, we can conclude that each numerator in the sum is bounded by a constant
C_{k}>0
. We can conclude that

\Big|\left(\dfrac{1}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{(i)}(x)\Big|\leq 4i\max_{k\leq i}C_{k}|x|^{-2},

raising to the power
j
leads to the conclusion.

\square

Lemma B.3
.

The map
t\mapsto\|\rho_{t}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M])}
is continuous.

Proof
.

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
t_{0}\in[0,1]
,

|\rho_{t}(x)^{-1}-\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{-1}|=\left|\dfrac{\delta tu_{t}(x)\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)\rho_{t}(x)}\right|\leq|\delta t|\dfrac{\|u_{t}\|_{\infty}\|\rho_{t_{0}}^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M])}}{\left(1-|\delta t|\|u_{t}\|_{\infty}\right)}

(106)

where
\delta t=t-t_{0}
and
u_{t}
is defined in (
80
) and is, by Corollary
6.8
, continuous with respect to
t
. Taking the supremum over
x\in[-M,M]
and let
t
goes to
t_{0}
establishes the result.

\square

Now it remains to bound the
L^{2}
or
L^{\infty}
norms of the functions
t\mapsto\mathfrak{f}^{(i)}
and
t\mapsto\mathcal{I}_{i}
used in the proofs of Theorems
4.13
,
4.14
and
4.17
.

Lemma B.4
.

The map
t\mapsto\|\mathcal{I}_{i}^{t}\|_{\infty}
for all
i\in\{1,2\}
is continuous where
\mathcal{I}_{i}^{t}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\mathcal{I}_{i}
is defined in (
54
) under the choice of potential
V_{G,\phi,t}
.

Proof
.

The fact that these maps are well-defined can be seen by a tail estimate. To show the continuity, let
x>0
,
t,t_{0}\in[0,1]
, by the mean-value theorem and with
u_{t}
defined in (
80
), we get

|\mathcal{I}_{1}^{t}(x)-\mathcal{I}_{1}^{t_{0}}(x)|=\Bigg|\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\rho_{t}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s-\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\Bigg|\\
\leq\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\left|\dfrac{1+\delta tu_{t}(s)}{1+\delta tu_{t}(x)}-1\right|\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\dfrac{|\delta t|\|u_{t}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{(1-|\delta t|\|u_{t}\|_{\infty})}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}(s-x)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s.

One thus concludes by the fact that
\delta t
goes to zero and that the following maps

x\in]0,+\infty[\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\displaystyle\int_{x}^{+\infty}(s-x)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptt\mapsto\|u_{t}\|_{W_{1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\hskip 28.45274pt\hskip 28.45274pt\hskip 28.45274pt

are bounded. For the second map, this follows from Corollary
6.8
. For the first one, while it is obviously bounded on a compact, the boundedness at infinity can be proven by an integration by parts:

\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}(s-x)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s=\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\left[\dfrac{\rho_{t_{0}}}{\rho_{t_{0}}^{\prime}}(s)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)(s-x)\right]_{x}^{+\infty}\\
-\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\left[\dfrac{\rho_{t_{0}}}{\rho_{t_{0}}^{\prime}}(s)+\left(\dfrac{\rho_{t_{0}}}{\rho_{t_{0}}^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime}(s)(s-x)\right]\rho_{t_{0}}(s)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s.

The first term in the right hand side is zero, while by assumption
(v)
, the last term is bounded by a tail-estimate shown by using Lemma
B.2
.
Doing the same thing over
]-\infty,0]
establishes that
t\mapsto\|\mathcal{I}_{1}^{t}\|_{\infty}
is continuous.

Just as before, we get by the mean-value theorem,

|\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t}(x)^{2}-\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t_{0}}(x)^{2}|\leq\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}^{2}(x)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\left|\dfrac{(1+\delta tu_{t}(s))^{2}}{(1+\delta tu_{t}(x))^{2}}-1\right|\rho_{t_{0}}(s)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq\dfrac{|\delta t|\|u_{t}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}(2+\|u_{t}\|_{\infty})}{(1-|\delta t|\|u_{t}\|_{\infty})^{2}}\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{2}}\int_{x}^{+\infty}(s-x)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s.

(107)

We conclude by showing that
x\in[0,+\infty[\mapsto\dfrac{1}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{2}}\displaystyle\int_{x}^{+\infty}(s-x)\rho_{t_{0}}(s)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}s
is bounded which can again be proven by the same integration by parts and by doing the exact same thing on
]-\infty,0]
. Therefore by the fact that

0\leq\bigg|\|\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t}\|_{\infty}^{2}-\|\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t_{0}}\|^{2}_{\infty}\bigg|\leq\|\left(\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t}\right)^{2}-\left(\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t_{0}}\right)^{2}\|_{\infty}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0

we conclude that
t\mapsto\|\mathcal{I}_{2}^{t}\|_{\infty}
is continuous.

\square

Next, we show that any polynomial in
\theta_{t}=(\log\rho_{t})^{\prime}
and its derivatives yield a continuous dependence in
t
.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
\mathsf{Q}
a polynomial in
\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(k)}
for some
k\geq 0
with coefficients independent of
t
, let
l\in\mathbb{N}
then then the two following maps are continuous:

(i)

t\mapsto\|\mathsf{Q}\big(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(k)}\big)\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M])}

(ii)

t\mapsto\|\sqrt{\rho_{t}^{(l)}}\mathsf{Q}\big(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(k)}\big)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

(iii)

t\mapsto\|\rho_{t}^{(l)}\mathsf{Q}\big(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(k)}\big)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

Proof
.

Proving these continuity results for any monomial in those variables is enough. Furthermore, since by continuity
x\in[-M,M]\mapsto\theta_{t}^{(i)}(x)
is uniformly bounded in
t
for all
i\leq k
, thus this monomial in
(\theta_{t}^{(i)})_{0\leq i\leq k}
converges uniformly to the monomial in
(\theta_{t_{0}}^{(i)})_{0\leq i\leq k}
as
t
goes to
t_{0}
. It comes from the fact that the product of two bounded, uniformly converging sequences of functions converges to the product of the limits and that for all
i>0
,
\theta_{t}^{(i)}(x)-\theta_{t_{0}}^{(i)}(x)=-\delta t\phi^{(i+1)}(x)-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{t}^{(i)}-\rho_{t_{0}}^{(i)}\right]
. The latter, when taking the supremum over
x\in[-M,M]
, goes to zero by Lemma
B.1
. This establishes
(i)
.
Furthermore, notice that
(iii)
implies
(ii)
since

\|\sqrt{\rho_{t}^{(l)}}\mathsf{Q}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta^{(k)}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}=\|\rho_{t}^{(l)}\mathsf{Q}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(k)}\right)^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{1/2}

and
\mathsf{Q}
is arbitrary so we only prove
(iii)
. Moreover since
\rho_{t}^{(l)}=\exp(\log\rho_{t})^{(l)}
can be written, by Faà di Bruno’s formula, as
\mathsf{R}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(l)}\right)\rho_{t}
where
\mathsf{R}
is a polynomial with coefficients independent of
t
, it suffices to prove the result for
l=0
.

For all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\theta_{t}^{(i)}(x)=-\delta_{i,0}x-t\phi^{(i+1)}(x)-2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{t}^{(i)}\right]
. Noticing, by Leibniz formula and the mean value theorem, that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
0<h<1
:

\bigg|x^{j}\rho_{t}(x)^{h}-x^{j}\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{h}\bigg|\leq\bigg|x^{j}\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{h}\bigg|.\Big|\left(1+\delta tu_{t}(x)\right)^{h}-1\Big|\\
\leq\dfrac{h|\delta t|\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{W_{l}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{\left(1-{|\delta t|\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{W_{l}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}\right)^{1-h}}\Big\|x\mapsto x^{j}\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{h}\Big\|_{\infty}

where the existence of the max is justified by Corollary
6.8
. Taking the supremum over
x\in\mathbb{R}
and let
t
goes to
t_{0}
shows that
t\mapsto\left(x\mapsto x^{j}\rho_{t}(x)^{h}\right)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
is continuous. By boundedness and continuity with respect to the
t
parameter of
t\mapsto\phi^{(i+1)}(x)+2P\mathcal{H}\left[\rho_{t}^{(i)}\right]
by Lemma
B.1
, we deduce that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha>0
,

\|\theta_{t}^{(i)}\rho_{t}(x)^{h}-\theta_{t_{0}}^{(i)}\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{h}\|_{\infty}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0.

From this last uniform convergence result, we show that by taking a monomial
\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(\theta^{(i)}_{t}\right)^{l_{i}}
such that
\sum_{i=0}^{k}l_{i}=m
, we deduce that
\rho_{t}\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(\theta^{(i)}_{t}\right)^{l_{i}}=\prod_{i=0}^{k}\left(\theta^{(i)}_{t}\sqrt[m]{\rho_{t}}\right)^{l_{i}}
, as a product of bounded, uniformly converging
t
-sequences of functions, it converges uniformly. This concludes the proof.

\square

To state the following lemma, we recall that
\alpha_{t}=\dfrac{\rho_{t}}{\rho_{t}^{\prime}}
.

Lemma B.6
.

[Continuity of
L^{\infty}
norms]
For all
j\in\{1,2,3,5,6\}
,
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M]^{c})}
is continuous where we recall that for
\bm{i}=(i_{1},i_{2})
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)\alpha_{t}(x)P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(x),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}_{l,\bm{i}}^{(2),t}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{t}(x)}\cdot\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\left[\alpha_{t}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(y)\rho_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(3),t}_{l,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{t}(x)}\cdot\Bigg(\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\alpha_{t}(y)^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)(y)^{2}\rho_{t}(y)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\Bigg)^{1/2},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(5),t}_{l,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{t}(x)}\cdot\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(y)\Big|\rho_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{(6),t}_{l,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{\Big|Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)\Big|}{\rho_{t}(x)}\cdot\Bigg(\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(y)\Big|^{2}\rho_{t}(y)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\Bigg)^{1/2}.

Proof
.

First, one can check that, from Lemma
4.12
,
\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}(x^{-1})
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Recalling the definition of
\alpha_{t}(x)
, there exists
n>0
and a polynomial expression
\mathsf{P}
with coefficients independent of
t
such that

\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)=\dfrac{\mathsf{P}\left(x^{-1},t\phi^{\prime},\dots,t\phi^{(k)},P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}],\dots,P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}^{(k-1)}]\right)}{(1+t\phi^{\prime}(x)x^{-1}+2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}](x)x^{-1})^{n}}.

where the numerator is a
\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}(x^{-1})
. Since,
\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}
is a bounded rational function such that the denominator is bounded from below uniformly in
t
(see Lemma
B.2
) and such that both the numerator and denominator converges uniformly, we conclude from this closed form, that
\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}
converges uniformly to
\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t_{0}}_{l,\bm{i}}
when
t
goes to
t_{0}
on
[-M,M]^{c}
. Thus,
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M]^{c})}
is continuous.

We only prove the continuity of
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M]^{c})}
in the case
j=5
, since the exact same arguments also prove the cases
j\in\{2,3,6\}
. Since
|\rho_{t}(x)-\rho_{t_{0}}(x)|\leq|\delta t|\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty}|\rho_{t_{0}}(x)
by (
80
) and Corollary
6.8
, and that the following map is uniformly bounded in
t\in[0,1]
and
x>M

g_{t}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{t}(x)}\int\limits_{x}^{+\infty}\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(y)\Big|\rho_{t_{0}}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y,

we can just show that
\|g_{t}-g_{t_{0}}\|_{\infty}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}0
. Moreover by (
106
),
it is enough to show the uniform convergence for
\dfrac{\rho_{t}}{\rho_{t_{0}}}g_{t}
. One can also notice that, for constants
C_{l,i_{1}}
and
C_{l,\bm{i}}
independents of
t
,

|Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)|\leq C_{l,i_{1}}|x|^{l-i_{1}}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{ and }\hskip 14.22636pt\Big|P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(y)\Big|\leq C_{l,\bm{i}}|x|^{-(l-i_{1})}.

Finally, by writing
|x^{{}^{-(l-i_{1})}}Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)|
as polynomial in
x^{-1}
,
t\phi^{(i+1)}(x)
and
\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}^{(i)}]
for
i\geq 0
and
x^{l-i_{1}}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(x)
as a rational function in those same variables, we conclude that these functions converge uniformly towards the same functions at
t_{0}
. Therefore

x\mapsto x^{-(l-i_{1})}Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)\dfrac{x^{l-i_{1}}}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)}\int\limits_{x}^{+\infty}\dfrac{\rho_{t_{0}}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y^{l-i_{1}}}\Big|y^{l-i_{1}}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})})(y)\Big|

converges uniformly to the same functions at
t_{0}
. This establishes the proposition.

\square

The final ingredient for showing the continuity with respect to
t
of the constants in Theorems
4.13
,
4.14
and
4.17
is the continuity of these
L^{2}
-norms.

Lemma B.7
.

[Continuity
L^{2}
-norms]
For all
j\in\llbracket 1,4\rrbracket
, the maps
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(j),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M,M]^{c})}
are continuous where

\mathfrak{f}^{(4),t}_{l,\bm{i}}:x\mapsto\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)}{\rho_{t}(x)}\cdot\Bigg|\int\limits_{x}^{\mathrm{sgn}(x)\infty}\left[\alpha_{t}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)\right]^{\prime}(y)\rho_{t}(y)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\Bigg|^{1/2}

(108)

and where
\mathfrak{f}^{(j),t}_{l,\bm{i}}
for
j=1,2,3
are defined in Lemma
B.6
.

Proof
.

For the continuity of
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M,M]^{c})}
, we use dominated convergence theorem. Since, we showed uniform convergence and that
\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{\infty}([-M,M]^{c})}<+\infty
, we conclude that for all
x\in[-M,M]^{c}
,

\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)^{2}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t_{0}}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)^{2}.

The domination follows from the fact
\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)=\underset{|x|\rightarrow\infty}{O}(x^{-2})
by Lemma
4.12
, and that all the dependence in
t
is bounded, hence there exists a constant
C_{l,\bm{i}}>0
independent of
t
such that, for all
x\in[-M,M]^{c}
and all
t\in[0,1]
,

|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}(x)|\leq\dfrac{C}{x^{2}}.

This establishes that
\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M,M]^{c})}\underset{t\rightarrow t_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\|\mathfrak{f}^{(1),t_{0}}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M,M]^{c})}
.

We now establish the continuity of
t\mapsto\|\mathfrak{f}^{(3),t}_{l,\bm{i}}\|_{L^{2}([-M,M]^{c})}
, the case
j\in\{2,4\}
can be obtained with the exact same arguments. We want to use dominated convergence theorem, for
\left({\mathfrak{f}^{(3),t}_{l,\bm{i}}}\right)^{2}
. The latter, when
t\rightarrow t_{0}\in[0,1]
, also converges uniformly since it is uniformly bounded and that we proved that
\mathfrak{f}^{(3),t}_{l,\bm{i}}
converges uniformly. It just remains to verify the domination hypothesis. By Lemma
4.12
, we know that
\alpha_{t}(y)^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)(y)^{2}=\underset{|y|\rightarrow\infty}{O}(y^{-2(l-i_{1}+1)}).
We conclude by Lemma
B.2
that there exists a constant
C_{l,\bm{i}}>0
such that for all
y>M
,

\alpha_{t}(y)^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)(y)^{2}\leq\dfrac{C_{l,\bm{i}}}{y^{2(l-i_{1}+1)}}.

Similarly,
\Big|Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)^{2}\Big|\leq C_{l,i_{1}}|x|^{2(l-i_{1})}
for all
x>M
and for
C_{l,i_{1}}>0
a constant independent of
t
and
x
. Finally, we get the following domination for an arbitrary
t_{0}
and all
x>M
:

\Bigg|\dfrac{Q_{i_{1}}^{l}\left(\theta_{t},\dots,\theta_{t}^{(i_{1})}\right)(x)^{2}}{\rho_{t}(x)^{2}}\int\limits_{x}^{+\infty}\alpha_{t}(y)^{2}P_{i_{2}}^{l-i_{1}}\left(\alpha_{t},\dots,\alpha_{t}^{(i_{2})}\right)(y)^{2}\rho_{t}(y)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\Bigg|\\
\leq\dfrac{C_{l,i_{1}}|x|^{2(l-i_{1})}}{\rho_{t_{0}}(x)^{2}(1-\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty})^{2}}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\dfrac{C_{l,\bm{i}}(1+\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty})^{2}\rho_{t_{0}}(y)^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y}{y^{2(l-i_{1}+1)}}.

The RHS is in
L^{1}([M,+\infty[)
by integration by parts as it was done in the proof of Lemma
B.4
. We conclude by doing the same on
]-\infty,-M]
.

\square

Proposition B.8
.

With the choice of potential
V_{\phi,t}
, the following map is continuous

t\in[0,1]\mapsto\Big(C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}),C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}),C_{H^{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})\Big)

Proof
.

By recalling the expression of those constants in (
52
), (
63
), (
70
), it is clear that they are continuous expressions of the
t
-continuous quantities considered in Lemmas
B.3
,
B.4
,
B.5
,
B.6
and
B.7
. The conclusion follows.

\square

B.2
Parameter-continuity of
C_{\mathcal{L}_{t}}
and
K_{V_{G,\phi,t}}

In
[
27
, App. A]
, the authors showed that for a general potential
V
, the operator
\mathcal{A}
considered as an unbounded operator on
\mathsf{H}
has the same spectrum as the Schrödinger operator
\mathcal{S}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{S})\rightarrow L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, defined by
\mathcal{S}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}-\Delta+w_{V}
with:

\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{S})\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\left\{u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}),uV^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),-u^{\prime\prime}+w_{V}u\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}),\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x=0\right\}

and

w_{V}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}V^{\prime 2}-V^{\prime\prime}+2PV^{\prime}\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]-2P\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}^{\prime}]+2P^{2}\mathcal{H}[\rho_{V}]^{2}\right)=\dfrac{1}{2}\Big[(\log\rho_{V})^{\prime\prime}+\dfrac{1}{2}(\log\rho_{V})^{\prime 2}\Big]\,.

Since
C_{\mathcal{L}}=\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{A})=\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S})
by Theorem
4.2
, we just have to show that when choosing the potential
V=V_{G,\phi,t}
, the
t
-dependent Schrödinger operator
\mathcal{S}_{t}
with potential
w_{t}\overset{(\mathrm{def})}{=}w_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
produces a continuous smallest eigenvalue
\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{t})
.

Proposition B.9
.

The map
t\in[0,1]\mapsto C_{\mathcal{L}_{t}}=\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{t})
is continuous.

Proof
.

First for all
t\in[0,1]
,
\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{t})>0
.
Secondly, we have the following equalities:

\lambda_{1}(\mathcal{S}_{t})=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{S}_{t})\\
\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=1\end{subarray}}\Braket{u,\mathcal{S}_{t}[u]}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\\
\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=1\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(u(x)^{\prime})^{2}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}}u(x)^{2}w_{t}(x)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x.

From the definition of
w_{t}
and Lemma
B.1
, it is easy to deduce that

\|w_{t}-w_{t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\underset{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}0.

Hence for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[0,1]
,
u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=1
, we have

\left|\Braket{u,\mathcal{S}_{t}[u]}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}-\Braket{u,\mathcal{S}_{t^{\prime}}[u]}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\right|\leq\|w_{t}-w_{t^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}

hence
\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\\
\|u\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left|\Braket{u,\mathcal{S}_{t}[u]}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}-\Braket{u,\mathcal{S}_{t^{\prime}}[u]}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}\right|\underset{t\rightarrow t^{\prime}}{\longrightarrow}0.
Since uniform convergence is enough to ensure convergence of infinimums, we get the result.

\square

We know prove the continuity of the constant
K_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
introduced in Theorem
2.3
.

Lemma B.10
.

The following map is continuous

t\mapsto K_{V_{G,\phi,t}}=2P\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}]\|_{\infty}+C+P\displaystyle\Big|\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\log|x-y|\rho_{t}(x)\rho_{t}(y)\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}x\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathrm{d}y\Big|

for
C
some fixed constant (independent of
t
).

Proof
.

We already proved the continuity of the map
t\mapsto\|\mathcal{H}[\rho_{t}]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}
in Lemma
B.1
so it just remains to show that the double integral is continuous with respect to
t
. We prove this by dominated convergence theorem. The function
(x,y)\mapsto\log|x-y|\rho_{t}(x)\rho_{t}(y)
converges almost everywhere to

(x,y)\mapsto\log|x-y|\rho_{t_{0}}(x)\rho_{t_{0}}(y)

as
t
goes to
t_{0}
. Furthermore we have the following domination
(x,y)
-almost everywhere

\Big|\log|x-y|\Big|\rho_{t}(x)\rho_{t}(y)\leq\Big|\log|x-y|\Big|(1+\max_{s\in[0,1]}\|u_{s}\|_{\infty})^{2}\rho_{t_{0}}(x)\rho_{t_{0}}(y).

This allows us to conclude on the continuity of
t\mapsto K_{V_{G,\phi,t}}
.

\square

Proposition B.11
.

The map
t\mapsto C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V_{G,\phi,t})
where
C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V)
is defined in (
76
) is integrable on
[0,1]
.

Proof
.

By the bounds on
C_{H^{n}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})
,
C_{W_{n}^{\infty}}(\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}}),C_{H^{n}}(\Theta^{(2)}\circ\widetilde{\Xi_{1}^{-1}})

in Theorems
4.13
,
4.14
and
4.16
in addition to the continuity results of Lemmas
B.9
and
B.10
, we conclude that
C_{\mathrm{rem}}(V_{G,\phi,t})\in L^{1}([0,1])
.

\square
